New `ZuiRampControl` (`Zui/Toolkit/ZuiRampControl.cs`) + `Z.Ramp(…)` + ONE `ZuiReflect` case (ahead of both the `List<>` and the nested-plain-class branches, and registered in `IsWideControl` so the strip gets its own line): a strip painted per-texel from the ramp's OWN `Eval` over a checker so per-stop alpha reads — the editing surface IS the preview, there is no second image to drift — a marker lane tinted with each stop's own colour, drag to move a stop (clamped between its neighbours), double-click the strip to insert one carrying the colour the ramp already evaluates there (so inserting never changes the picture), right-click a marker to remove it, click a marker for a `Z.Popover` with `Z.Color` + a `Pos` MicroSlider + Remove, an always-present `+` (an empty ramp is LEGAL — Pyre's soot ramp means "no soot" — so the control never auto-seeds, and the `+` is the way back in), and the blend space as a `Z.Segmented`, never a dropdown. Type-level, so all five authored ramp fields adopt it at once with no per-form edit: `JetSettings.ramp` + `sootRamp` (inherited by Jet / RadialJet / ExplosiveJet = 23 variant boxes), `OrbForm.StyleSettings.ramp`, and PlasmaBloom's `hueA` / `hueB`. `ZuiReflect` also collapses a list ELEMENT that is nothing but 2–5 bounded scalars (`[Range]` float/int or bool, nothing else, no `[ZUIShowIf]`/`[ZUIPair2D]`) to ONE row — `#N` · dials inline · `×` — instead of a header row above a body row; the live case is `ExplosiveJetSettings.blasts`. Presentation only: `stops` / `space` keep their exact names, types, order and defaults (`PyreRamp`'s `IZuiRamp` members are thin forwarders adding no serialized state), `PyreShade.EvalStops` / `Blend` / `BakeLut` and every form's rendering math are untouched, so every authored asset loads byte-identically. NOT migrated to `ZuiGradient`/`Gradient` on purpose: Unity's `Gradient` caps at 8 colour keys (`PyreRampPresets.Ember()` has 10), blends gamma values where `PyreRamp` blends in linear light, and keeps alpha keys at independent positions where a `PyreRampStop`'s alpha IS its own opacity ceiling. And no "number of stops" slider — that is the bug `450de881` removed. `+6 PyreRampControlTests` (round-trip, sorted insert, insert-is-visually-neutral over an 11-point sweep, remove, the zero-stop state, and Eval parity with `PyreRamp.Evaluate` at the same eleven sample points the Kiln parity harness uses, for `Ember()` / `OrbFrost()` / `PlasmaIon()`).
- **SpriteFx — "Fake light" (T-0069): a sprite is lit by a nearby light with no normal map and no renderer lighting.** `Runtime/SpriteFx/SpriteFxRelight.cs` adds `RelightModifier` (a whole-frame effect, so it appears in the Add-effect picker's "Whole frame" section automatically) and `SfxRelief`, the pure shading math behind it. There is no real lighting anywhere: a fake SURFACE is invented from the sprite's own content — `SpriteFxAuxMap.BuildMap` supplies the height field, so the choice of source is the choice of character (edge-distance inflates the silhouette into a rounded volume, alpha makes a flat slab so only the outline catches light — a rim light, luma sculpts the brightness the artist already painted) — then differentiated into a per-pixel normal and shaded with Lambert + Blinn specular against either a directional light or a point light with a position, a height above the plane, a reach and a falloff. Because the surface is derived from the pixels, it follows the art and animates with it for free, with nothing to author and nothing to keep in sync. The light reaches the sprite in three independently zeroable ways: it modulates the existing colour (form), pulls it toward the light's own colour (tint), and adds on top only where the light actually falls (the flash dial) — the motivating case being a muzzle flash that throws light back onto the gun and the arm for a frame or two. It never writes outside the silhouette (outward reach 0) and never touches a fully transparent pixel. Point-light positions are in half-canvas units measured on the SHORTER half-extent, so a radius means the same distance horizontally and vertically on a non-square frame. The shading half deliberately mirrors `PyreField.ReliefLight` (same clamp-to-edge central difference, same 0.35 + light × 1.15 spread) but is re-implemented rather than referenced, since SpriteFx must not depend on Pyre. Deterministic (no `UnityEngine.Random`, no `Mathf.PerlinNoise`). New test assembly `Assets/Tests/SpriteFx/` — 7 tests covering flat-field uniformity, which slope a light picks out and that it swaps with the light, point attenuation to the ambient floor past the radius, the all-zero no-op, transparent-pixel safety and byte-level determinism.
- **ZUI — `ZuiSectionToggleBar` (T-0065): a full-width bar that shows/hides a whole `ZuiSection` at once, as an opt-in ALTERNATIVE to folding each section's own header — the two are mutually exclusive via a leading Sections/Toggle Bar mode switch, so a section is never fought over by two controls (`ZuiSection.HeaderFoldDisabled` disables the header's own fold click while the bar owns it). Mode choice persists per tool (EditorPrefs). Reusable across every `ZuiSection` user (currently 8 tools); PyrePlus adopts it first, over its six dial sections, the asset Tags section (which `ZuiAssetWindow<T>` now builds as a proper `ZuiSection` with a green header — exposed as the new protected `TagsSection` — instead of a bare island), and its own saved-Views bar (now wrapped in a `ZuiSection` too, so it folds and toggles exactly like the rest), sitting above the dial pane/preview split so it spans the whole window. PyrePlus's unused "Import from Pyre…" converter (slice 9) was removed in the same pass — `PyrePlusWindow.Import.cs` and `PyreToPlusConverter.cs` are gone.
- **Mirage as a testing ground: an editor can hand Mirage a character and ask for controls to drive it.** Three pieces. `ZoeCapabilities.Derive(zoe)` (`Runtime/ZoetropeLaunimator/`) reads what a Zoe can be ASKED to do — aim (and the exact directions its art supports), move, fire (and which weapons), which clips — entirely from existing authoring: aim/move via `MotionPoseCatalog.Derive` (so mirroring, per-rule channel overrides and composites are handled for free), firing from the weapon slots, clips from the version. Deliberately derived, never a new authored asset: a capability list that could disagree with the thing it describes is worse than none. `MirageManualDriver` (`Runtime/Mirage/`) is a THIRD driver alongside player input and an AI brain, not a special case of either — it publishes a full `MotionState` override rather than translating the transform, so the character walks IN PLACE and stays framed, and it keeps travel direction separate from aim so the backpedal case (walk east, face west) can actually be previewed. `MirageHud` grows a controls panel generated from those capabilities — a facing slider that SNAPS to the directions the art actually has (a pose between two authored frames isn't something the character can do, so the control won't express it), walk + "Move = facing" toggles, and fire.
  **Opt-in, requested by the configuring editor**: `PreviewableEntry.manualControls` → `MirageSubject.manualControls`, so a view assembled to look at an effect or a backdrop never grows character controls, while the Zoe editor's "Preview in Mirage" turns it on because that view exists precisely to put a character through its paces. What the controls ARE is not decided by the requester — the HUD derives them, so a Zoe with no weapon gets no fire toggle. The point of the split: nothing anywhere asks "is this the player" — a character declares what it can do and anything may drive it, which is what makes hand-testing an ENEMY need no special case. Verified end-to-end on ProtoGuy (16 directions, walk, `ProtoGuy Gun`), with a bare Zoe correctly yielding an empty control set rather than a broken panel.
- **Launimator — `DirectionMode.Rotation`: a sheet whose FRAMES are the directions, replacing 16 zones and 16 clip copies with arithmetic.** A 16-way rotation is a regular structure — frame *i* faces *i* × (360/N) — so nothing about it needs authoring, naming or storing. The set holds ONE member (the sheet); N is read from that sheet's own `frames.Count`, so the direction count can never drift out of step with the art (re-bake at 32 directions and the mapping follows with no edit). `LauminationResolution` gains `FrameIndex`/`HasFrame` — stored offset by one so `default`/`None` reads as -1 rather than frame 0, which a bare int would have made look like a valid request to hold frame 0 of nothing. `ZonedAnimationPlayer.EnterAtFrame(int)` parks on one frame and stops advancing (a sheet is a set of poses, not a cycle), `IZonedView.TryEnterFrame` exposes it, and `MotionPoseAnimator` dedups on (clip, zone, frame) advancing `_lastFrame` only on a CONFIRMED entry — the same rule `_lastZone` already followed, so a failed entry retries instead of sticking. `MotionPoseCatalog`'s key gains the frame too: without it every direction of a rotation sheet shares one clip name, an empty zone, no flip and no rotation, so all 16 probes would dedup to a SINGLE entry and the pose picker would offer one pose for a 16-way character.
  **Why, measured:** `LauminationSetMember.laumination` is a `Laumination` VALUE, not a reference, so every member serializes a complete copy of its clip — recipe, frames and full zone table. The existing `zoneName` "rotation sheet" technique removed the need for N separate clip ASSETS but not that cost: ProtoGuy's 16-way torso and 16-way idle legs held **16 copies each of one 16-frame sheet, plus 256 zone records**, to express what is really "frame i faces i × 22.5°". Migrating both sets took `ProtoGuy_draft.asset` from **12,955 lines to 1,808 (−86%)**: 35 members → 5, 40 embedded lauminations → 10, 544 zone records → 0. Purely additive — `Members` (genuinely different clips, e.g. a 3-way walk cycle with mirroring) is untouched and still the right mode for that, `zoneName` still works, and every pre-existing mode reports `HasFrame == false` and goes down exactly the old path. 8 new tests (34/34 in `LauminationSetResolverTests`, 12/12 in `MotionPoseTests`).
- **Zoetrope + Launimator — legs can steer by AIM and walk BACKWARDS.** A top-down shooter's legs shouldn't follow travel direction: aiming west while moving east is a backpedal, not an eastward walk. Two halves. Data — a part's Moving rule can now use `overrideChannel → Aim` (ProtoGuy's Legs does; its Idle rule already did, which is why idle looked right and walking didn't). Code — `ZonedAnimationPlayer.reversed` steps a plain strip backwards and wraps at 0 (verified: forward `012301`, reversed `032103`), surfaced as `IAnimatedView.SetPlaybackReversed`, and `MotionPoseAnimator.ShouldBackpedal()` drives it from `dot(state.heading, state.aim) < 0`. **Gated on the matched rule's effective channel being `Aim`** — a part still steering by Heading always faces the way it moves, so it is never backpedalling and must never be reversed. Reverse applies only to a plain strip; a zoned strip's zones are ordered ranges with their own behaviours, so stepping backwards through them has no defined meaning and it always advances forward. Known limit, stated rather than faked: perpendicular movement (strafing) plays forward — with only forward-facing walk art there is no sideways cycle to pick, and true strafing needs its own art, not a playback trick.
- **ZoeCharacter — `ZoeWeaponDriver`, the missing input→trigger bridge.** `IZoeInputSource.FirePressed()` / `FireHeld()` / `FireReleased()` existed with ZERO consumers anywhere in the package: every fire call site was bespoke demo code (`ShmupPlayer`, `GalleryDirector`, `ColosseumAgentBody`) or Mirage, so a Zoe with an equipped weapon had no way to pull its trigger from real input. `Runtime/ZoeCharacter/ZoeWeaponDriver.cs` reads the trigger and calls `ProjectileWeapon.TryFire()`; that is its whole job. It resolves neither direction nor spawn position — `TryFire()` already prefers a sibling `IVectorAimSource`'s animation-drawn barrel angle (a painted Vector MetaLayer, via Zoetrope's `MuzzleVectorTracker`) over raw aim, and spawns from the muzzle Transform that same tracker keeps live-positioned. `TriggerMode` is `FollowWeapon` (default — defers to the equipped weapon's own `automatic` flag, whose doc calls it "a property, not a behaviour" precisely so something like this reads it), `Press`, `Hold`, or `Release` (a bow/charged shot, the first consumer `FireReleased` has ever had). **Deliberately perspective-agnostic and a SIBLING of `TopDownMotionDriver`, not part of it**: locomotion is perspective-specific (a platformer needs gravity and a jump), but everything downstream of `Combatant.aimDirection` is not, so a platformer, side-scroller, twin-stick and light-gun game reuse this driver verbatim and replace only the motion half.
- **Combat2D — `IActiveWeaponSource`.** "Whichever weapon I currently have equipped", so a trigger mechanism needn't know how many slots a character has or which is switched in. Implemented by `Laubrary.Zoetrope.WeaponSwitcher` (its existing `ActiveWeapon` property already had exactly this shape and this stated purpose). Lives in Combat2D rather than Zoetrope for the same reason `IVectorAimSource` does — Zoetrope already depends on Combat2D, so the reverse reference would be circular — which is what lets the input layer reach the equipped weapon while depending only on Combat2D. A character with no switcher needs nothing: `ZoeWeaponDriver` falls back to a `ProjectileWeapon` found in its children.
- **SUPERSEDED, never shipped — reverted in `450de881` (2026-08-21); kept here only so the attempt is not re-made.** `PlusBands`, `IZuiBands` and `ZuiBandsControl` were all deleted: `PlusBands.SetCount` destructively split/merged its own stop list, so shrinking the band count then regrowing it lost hand-picked colours. Hard band tables are now a `ZuiGradient` locked via `ZuiGradient.LockToBands(count)`, and no stop list gets a count slider. Original entry: **PyrePlus + ZUI — a bespoke bands control replaces the per-stop cards for hard band tables (T-0064).** New `PlusBands` (`Runtime/PyrePlus/PlusBands.cs`): N hard cel bands as a sorted list of (threshold, colour) stops — the same serialized shape as `PlusRamp`, so a saved layer loads with no migration and the contract's repeated-position step encoding collapses to one entry per band on load; `Thresholds` / `Colors32` cached on `ContentHash()` for `PlusShade.Banded`, `Eval`, `SetCount` (grow splits the widest band copying its colour, shrink removes the narrowest, band 0 stays), `SetThreshold` (clamped between neighbours), `FromStops` / `ToStops` (lossless), `transparentBelowFirst` for a form whose own rule cuts pixels under the floor. New `IZuiBands` (ZuiRuntime) + `ZuiBandsControl` (`Zui/Toolkit/ZuiBandsControl.cs`) and ONE `ZuiReflect` case: a field of any `IZuiBands` type draws as ONE row — a "Bands" count `Z.MicroSlider` beside a stepped strip painted from the table's own `Eval` over a checker (the editor surface is the quantised preview), markers under the strip drag a threshold, a click on a band opens a `Z.Popover` with its colour and threshold; Undo / dirty / per-layer cache re-key through the same hooks as every reflected field. `Z.MicroSlider` gains an optional `onBeforeMutate`. Adopted by Torch (`TorchSettings.ramp`, presets `PlusRampPresets.TorchBands(name)`; the contract-verbatim `Torch*()` step ramps remain the source) and Arc Burst (`bands` → `palette`, `[FormerlySerializedAs]`; `ArcBands.*` build `PlusBands`, the renderer now honours any authored count instead of padding to 5). Presentation only: all 15 contract draws (Torch 5, Arc 10) byte-identical to the T-0063 baseline and 15/15 comparer PASS; suite 207/207 (+9 `PlusBandsTests`). Not a Unity `Gradient`: 8-key cap, and Fixed mode colours a band by the key at its END.
- **PyrePlus — envelope vetting on the ported Kiln forms (T-0063).** Every dial of `PlasmaBloomForm` was classified by where its algorithm reads it; the per-frame AMOUNTS became `[Range] ZUIValue` envelopes (Static renders byte-identically — all 10 contract draws re-dump with the same FNV and PASS every parity stage; a Curve drives the dial over the layer's life), resolved once per frame by `Prepare` into a `live` snapshot the algorithm reads; counts / seeds / periods / enums / per-piece terms, timing constants and rates, and the fit's inputs stay plain. Plasma's fit pre-pass resolves the envelopes at each clock sample it measures (`PlusPlasmaBloom.Solve(…, liveAt)`), so an animated blast is still contained. Tests: `Assets/Tests/PyrePlus/EnvelopeTestUtil.cs` + two per form (flat Curve == Static; a ramp changes the frame; the fit follows the envelope). Arc Burst: 49 animatable (the alpha mapping, bloom, stroke scales, and per layout the roughness / probabilities / opacities / jitter / depth shading / branch geometry / flash), all 10 draws byte-identical + PASS. Orb: 77 animatable (placement, the shared tone map / nose / glow, and every variant amplitude / exponent / radius / width), all 5 draws byte-identical + PASS. Torch: 46 animatable (placement / reach, the width / falloff / ceiling / root profile, every breathing / sway / pulse / noise / glow / tongue / ember amplitude, cooling, alpha thresholds, ramp top), all 5 draws byte-identical + PASS, 86/86 keys. Jet 33 / Radial Jet 38 animatable (placement, aim / reach / cone, puff size, strength / cooling / soot, pulse / sweep / shock / root / ring / warp amounts, the shade thresholds; the radial bias / birth radius / swirl / lobes), all 13 draws byte-identical, PASS on every draw but the documented wyrm contract gap. `JetSettings` / `TorchSettings.EnsureLive()` serves a direct caller the Static values. Suite 198 / 198.
- **PyrePlus — Port 05: Kiln Flame / agent3_fork_explosive THE DETONATION (gen 7) as `ExplosiveJetForm`, on the shared jet engine; supersedes Fork Blast (T-0049).** `Runtime/PyrePlus/Forms/Kiln/Jet/ExplosiveJetProgram.cs` is `ExplosiveJetSettings : JetSettings` (141 contract keys: the radial ten, the blast schedule as `List<ExplosiveBlast>` cards, `blastSpan` / `blastSkew` / `blastFront` / `velSpread` / `swell`, the death `hold` / `shrink` / `shrinkAt` / `leadDie` / `opaq` / `shedSwell`, and the concern boxes `fracture` / `fracture2` / `flash` / `chunks` / `gobs` / `dust`) + `ExplosiveJetProgram : JetProgram` overriding `BuildSlots` (blast groups, per-block stratified angles, skewed births, front, velocity tail, rank lead, the plates), `Warp` (polar), `Root` (blast envelope), `Emit` (whole: hold, outside-in shrink, lead_die, swell, the crack and the second crack, shed swell), `Rings` (blast-timed `ring_arc` fronts that break with the gas), `Sparks` (blast-timed) and `Frame` (Flash / Chunks / Gobs / Dust inserted), with `ExplosiveJetShade` for `opaq`. `Jet/ExplosiveJetDraws.cs` holds the ten contract parameter sets GENERATED from params.json (fitted `hi` / `curve` frozen per draw); `Jet/ExplosiveJetForm.cs` (group "Kiln/Flame") has a `variant` (Detonate / Backdraft / Chain / Frag / Fuelair / Lash / Muzzle / Shatter / Shockfront / Starshell) with one `[ZUIShowIf]`-gated box each (default Detonate, the draw the generation was tuned on), a contract loader for the schedule arrays and the boxed keys, and a swarm that hands each particle ONE blast of the schedule centred on it. Parity on the ten contract draws: **PASS every stage on all ten on the first run** — field MAD ≤ 1.8e-4, IoU ≥ 0.9998, alpha census identical, ramp ΔE ≤ 0.98, pixel rgb MAD ≤ 0.055 (the old Fork Blast baseline against `detonate`: IoU 0.089, ramp ΔE 44, rgb MAD 57 — every stage FAIL). 2.3–5.9 ms a frame at 128 px. `PlusRampPresets.JetCordite()` / `JetCorditeSoot()` / `JetToxic()` added (`Jet("CORDITE")` / `Jet("TOXIC")`). Engine: `JetSlots` unsealed, `JetSettings.ShallowClone()`, `hi` range to 16, `JetFormBase.Shader` / `InstanceSettings` virtuals — no arithmetic touched; Jet + Radial re-dump byte-identical. Tests: `Assets/Tests/PyrePlus/ExplosiveJetTests.cs`.
- **PyrePlus — Port 06: Kiln Flame / agent3_fork_radial THE RADIAL JET (gen 3) as `RadialJetForm`, on the shared jet engine (T-0050).** `Runtime/PyrePlus/Forms/Kiln/Jet/RadialJetProgram.cs` is `RadialJetSettings : JetSettings` (the ten gen-3 keys as dials: `bias`, `srcR`, `swirl`, `spin`, `lobes`, `lobeDepth`, `lobeKick`, `rootK`, `ringFlat`, `warpSpin`) + `RadialJetProgram : JetProgram` overriding the engine's virtual stages — `BuildSlots` (bias power, stratified + permuted angles for wide arcs, the crossing-free lobes remap, lobe_kick), `Warp` (the POLAR warp: noise in (θ, r) scrolled outward by whole radial periods, one turn = one lattice period, radial / tangential displacement faded at the centre), `Root` (a burner RING of root_k lumps, or a round lump), `Emit` (spin, the room-bounded shed kick dropped on a full circle, src_r birth radius, swirl with the lead-angle orientation), `Rings` (face-on `ring_flat` rings; edge-on delegates to the base), `Sparks` — no new engine seam was needed and the base engine's arithmetic is untouched (the five Jet contract draws re-dump byte-identical). `Jet/RadialJetDraws.cs` holds the eight contract parameter sets; `Jet/RadialJetForm.cs` (group "Kiln/Flame") has a `variant` (Corona / Fan / Crown / Whirl / Shockring / Maw / Starburst / Halo) with one `[ZUIShowIf]`-gated box each (default Corona, the MANIFEST's "plain answer"). Parity on the eight contract draws: **PASS every stage on all eight on the first run** — field MAD ≤ 1.4e-7, IoU 1.000, alpha census identical, ramp ΔE ≤ 0.98, pixel rgb MAD ≤ 0.042. 5.7–9.6 ms a frame at 128 px. Swarm = one radial jet per particle centred on it.
- **`JetFormBase`** (`Jet/JetFormBase.cs`): the jet family's shared form plumbing — placement dials (`anchorX` / `anchorY` / `scale` / `swarmSize`), solo + swarm instancing through `JetFrame`, the LUT cache, the field publisher / ramp probe, pixel modifiers, `SetContractParam` / `ResolveGeometry`. `JetForm` is now a thin subclass (its variant, five boxes, `Active` / `Program` / `TrySetVariant`); a future family member (the explosive fork) adds the same three members and nothing else. Card order for both forms: placement grid, then Variant, then the box.
- **`PlusRampPresets.JetBurner` / `JetBurnerTips` / `JetWhirl` / `JetSolar` / `JetGhost`**, and `Jet(name)` / `JetSecondary(name)` / `JetSootWindow(name)` extended with BURNER / WHIRL / VIOLET (stop for stop the wyrm ramp) / SOLAR / GHOST; `JetSettings.slots` range 1–800 and `ringAmp` 0–4 (family-wide); tests: 15 new in `Assets/Tests/PyrePlus/RadialJetTests.cs` (168/168).
- **PyrePlus — Port 07: Kiln Flame / agent3 THE DIRECTIONAL JET (gen 2) as `JetForm`, on a shared jet ENGINE (T-0051).** `Runtime/PyrePlus/Forms/Kiln/Jet/PlusJetEngine.cs` is the agent3 family's `flame3` core ported once — `JetSettings` (every contract key as a dial, not sealed), `JetProgram` (the stages Warp → Root → Emit → Rings → Sparks as virtual methods on a stateless program; helpers `LeadRank` / `StratifiedPermuted` ready for the forks), `JetField.Blob` / `JetNoise` (tileable value noise on a wrapping lattice), `JetShade` (a 1024-entry LUT baked exactly like `lut.build_lut` — linear-light interpolation, rounded once through uint8 sRGB — per-stop opacity ceilings, the soot two-ramp smoothstep crossfade, `steps` quantisation of t only, the edge smoothstep, despeckle; `CeilingExponent` hook for the explosive fork's `opaq`); its header carries the hand-off note Ports 06 (radial) and 05 (explosive) start from. `Jet/JetDraws.cs` holds the five contract parameter sets; `Jet/JetForm.cs` (group "Kiln/Flame") has a `variant` (Lance / Gout / Sputter / Whip / Wyrm) with one `[ZUIShowIf]`-gated `JetSettings` box each (default Gout, the flagship), shared `anchorX` / `anchorY` / `scale` and `swarmSize`. Runs in the draw's source-px frame sampled through the canvas (scale-invariant field, so the contract's fitted `lo` / `hi` / `curve` hold at 64 px), loops seamlessly (phase = frame / frameCount, noise scroll snapped per axis). numpy streams exact via `PlusNumpyRng` (slot table, sparks, every noise lattice); parity on the five contract draws: four PASS every stage on the first run, wyrm passes all but the contract's own `alpha_levels_min` (its sheet has 150, the threshold says 200 — logged as a contract gap); field MAD ≤ 7.6e-8, IoU 1.000, alpha census identical, pixel rgb MAD ≤ 0.040. `UsesFill` false; swarm = one jet per particle turned by its orientation, heats summing; 1.6–3.5 ms a frame at 128 px.
- **`PlusRampPresets.JetTorch / JetDirty(+Soot) / JetGold(+Heat) / JetWyrm` + `Jet(name)` / `JetSecondary(name)` / `JetSootWindow(name)`** (the jet family's ramps with their per-stop opacity ceilings and crossfade windows; EMBER stays `Ember()` / `EmberSoot()`), **`PlusNumpyRng.Shuffle(double[])`**; tests: 15 new in `Assets/Tests/PyrePlus/JetTests.cs` (153/153).
- **PyrePlus — Port 04: Kiln Flame / agent2 the GROUNDED flame (gen 2) as `TorchForm` (T-0048).** `Runtime/PyrePlus/Forms/Kiln/TorchForm.cs` + `PlusTorch.cs`, group "Kiln/Flame": a torch / brazier / campfire licking upward from a fixed fuel bed — an anchored envelope plus periodic 3-D Perlin fBm (ridged for licks, billow for lumps) on a 3× supersampled grid, a divergence-free curl warp, a surge envelope with a lump of heat riding up the column, a whip whose wave travels up the flame, tongue and ember populations summed into the heat, a hard 7-band cel palette on the heat cooled with height and a continuous smoothstep alpha, box-filtered in premultiplied space. The five published draws are one program with five parameter sets, so the form has a `variant` (Emberbed / Surge / Barbs / Curl / Lash — calm → violent) and one `[ZUIShowIf]`-gated settings box per variant, every box the same 74-dial `TorchSettings` with that draw's contract values as defaults (default variant Barbs, the 0115 torch); shared dials are the placement (`axisX`, `ground`, `height` as canvas fractions) and `swarmSize`. Runs in the draw's source-px frame magnified to the dialled height, loops seamlessly over the clip's frame count (t = frame / frameCount). numpy streams replicated exactly (the perm table via `PlusNumpyRng.Shuffle`, tongues and embers via `Uniform`); parity PASS on all five contract draws on the first run (field MAD ≤ 2e-7, IoU 1.000, ramp ΔE 0, pixel MAD ≤ 0.013). `UsesFill` false (the palette is the colour); swarm = one flame per particle, heats summing; 70–147 ms a frame at 128 px (logged as a Burst candidate).
- **`PlusFieldOps.GradientNoise3Periodic`** (classic periodic Perlin gradient noise over a supplied permutation table, wrap on the lattice index) and **`PlusRampPresets.TorchHot / TorchEmber / TorchWhite / TorchRim / TorchGold` + `Torch(name)` + `TorchTop(name)`** (the flame agent's banded palettes as the contract's stepped stops), with tests (13 new in `Assets/Tests/PyrePlus/TorchTests.cs`, 138/138).
- **ZUI — a `[Range]`-bounded `Vector2` field reflects as ONE `Z.MinMax` range slider** (x = low, y = high) instead of two scrub fields — the layout rule that a min/max pair is never two separate controls, fixed in `ZuiReflect` so every reflected pair benefits (the Torch's tongue / ember ranges are the first). An unbounded Vector2 draws as before.
- **PyrePlus — Port 03: Kiln Energy Projectile / agent2 THE ORB (gen 2) as `OrbForm` (T-0047).** `Runtime/PyrePlus/Forms/Kiln/OrbForm.cs` + `PlusOrb.cs`, group "Kiln/Energy Projectile": a compact glowing ball with a trailing wake, travelling +x — one additive energy field of compact-support kernels and looping plane-wave turbulence, speed-smeared and softened, read through the draw's sRGB ramp by tone = 1 − exp(−E·gain) with a gradient alpha window, a floor cut and a despeckle. The five published draws are five programs, so the form has a `variant` (Emberdrift / Wisp / Coronal / Membrane / Voltcore) and one `[ZUIShowIf]`-gated settings box per variant holding that draw's style, smear, glow and components as dials named after the contract's keys (defaults = the contract values, HARDNESS 0.40 folded in); shared dials are the placement (`noseX`, `axisY`, `radius` as canvas fractions, `wake` in core radii) and the finish (`softenPasses`, `floor`, `despeckle`, `despeckleBelow`). The programs run in the archetype's own source-px frame magnified to the dialled radius, so the 64 px orb is the 192 px orb at pixel scale. numpy `default_rng` streams replicated exactly (`PlusNumpyRng.Uniform / Integers / ChoiceSign` — Lemire's bounded integers on buffered 32-bit halves); parity PASS on all five contract draws on the first run (field Pearson 1.000, IoU 1.000, pixel MAD ≤ 0.032). Swarm = one orb per instance; `UsesFill` false (the ramp is the colour); 1.4–2.1 ms a frame at 128 px.
- **`PlusFieldOps.SmearShiftX`** (whole-pixel −x shift smear with geometric weights, zero-filled, normalise on/off — the Kiln "speed smudge") and **`PlusFieldOps.BinomialBlur`** (zero-padded [¼ ½ ¼] separable soften), with tests. **`PlusRampPresets.OrbEmber / OrbFrost / OrbGold / OrbToxin / OrbVolt` + `Orb(name)`**: the orb signatures verbatim (8 sRGB control points each).
- **`PlusParityDump.DumpOptions.cropW / cropH`**: dump the centred window of the square canvas, for contracts whose frames are not square (the orb's 192 × 96) — off by default.

### Fixed
- **A character with no death animation was destroyed the instant it died, so `deathLinger` never ran (T-0090).** `ReactionFxPlayer.OnDeath` announces `DeathFinished` synchronously when there is nothing to play, and `ZoeState` subscribes to it — but `ZoeSpawner` adds `ReactionFxPlayer` BEFORE `ZoeState`, so that synchronous signal arrived (and disposed the body) before `ZoeState.OnDied` had even run to schedule the linger. `ZoeState` now only disposes on `DeathFinished` when it is actually waiting for a death clip (`_awaitingDeathClip`), which is set from the new `ReactionFxPlayer.DeathClipArmed`. That flag reports whether a clip GENUINELY started, not whether one was configured: `TryArmClip` used to return true on the strength of a non-empty clip name alone and discard `IAnimatedView.PlayClip`'s real result, so a death clip whose name the view did not know armed "successfully", never completed, and left the body standing forever — the mirror-image bug, now closed by the same change. `AnimatedViewRelay.PlayClip` also stopped recording a completion callback for a clip that was refused, which used to fire it at the end of whatever WAS playing.
- **A revived character could never be hit again (T-0091).** `ZoeState.OnDied` disables every child `Collider2D`, and nothing turned them back on — `Health.Revive()` wrote fields and fired no C# event, so no listener could react. `Health` now has a `Revived` event (plus an `onRevived` UnityEvent for designer wiring) fired at the end of `Revive()`, and `ZoeState` records exactly which colliders IT disabled and re-enables that set on revival — never "enable everything", which would switch on colliders another system deliberately disabled (an unequipped weapon slot, the inactive projectile template). Revival also cancels the pending disposal timer, clears the dead/disposing/stun flags, and (in `ReactionFxPlayer`) releases the death animation's arbiter claim, without which a respawned body stayed frozen on its last death frame. **Correction:** that last clause did not actually hold as first shipped — the release was written but could never run, because the token it needed had already been thrown away; see the revived-character-animation-locked entry below, which is what makes it true. Mirage's Target Practice, the shipped tool this broke, additionally now sets `ZoeState.disposal = Leave` on its dummy: the default 1.5s death linger is shorter than the default 2s respawn delay, so the object being revived was destroyed first, taking its own respawn coroutine with it.
- **An interrupted reaction was dropped silently — no completion signal, and its pending effects were lost (T-0094).** `AnimationArbiter` is now attached to EVERY character by `ZoeSpawner` (it used to exist only on characters with an authored directional MotionPose, with a single consumer), and reactions, plain movement and named states all route through it instead of calling the view directly. It carries an explicit priority ladder — locomotion 0, named state 100, hurt 200, death 1000 — that `LocomotionAnimator` and `MotionPoseAnimator` now reference rather than restating, and equal priority no longer steals: a newcomer must strictly outrank an active claim, so a second reaction cannot cut off one still playing. Claims are rolled back when the view refuses the clip (previously a claim on a clip that never played locked the arbiter permanently), expiry is honoured the moment it passes rather than at the next `Update`, and a same-owner/same-clip re-claim reasserts without restarting. `ReactionFxPlayer` reports the outcome: `HurtFinished`/`DeathFinished` now carry a `wasInterrupted` bool, `EventFinished` carries `(id, wasInterrupted)`, and a new `EventRefused` fires when a declared event's clip was outranked (`Raise`'s bool return keeps its original "no such event" meaning, which `CueRelay` and `WeaponMuzzleCue` depend on). An interrupted reaction now flushes its unfired `OnFrame` effects before ending, so being cut short costs it its remaining timing, never its effects. A death claims the body permanently and never releases, so nothing animates over a corpse. `LocomotionAnimator.SuppressFor` (zero callers, a second silent arbitration channel) is deleted. **An adversarial re-trace of this work returned eight further defects, fixed in the entries immediately below — treat this bullet as the first half of the change, not the whole of it.**
- **A composite Zoe could never play a reaction clip at all, and its arbiter arbitrated nothing (T-0094 follow-up).** `CompositeLauminaryView.Build` added only `CompositeZonedPlayer`, which is an `IPartLookup` and NOT an `IAnimatedView` — so a composite character's root had no animated view whatsoever. Every consumer that discovers one by `GetComponent` silently got null: `ReactionFxPlayer` reported "nothing to play" for hurt, for death and for every named `Raise()` no matter what was authored; its frame handler was never subscribed, so only `Immediate` FX entries fired and every `OnFrame` entry was dead code; the root `AnimationArbiter` that `ZoeSpawner` attaches had no view to play through, so every claim rolled back and it policed nothing; and an effect placed at a painted `MetaPoint` fell back to the sprite's bounds centre because there was nothing to sample the layer from. This was the state of the project's own demo character, ProtoGuy. New `CompositeAnimatedView` is the whole body's `IAnimatedView`: a clip plays on **every part that knows it** and leaves the others alone (which is what makes "walking legs under a firing torso" fall out for free), it reports frames only from the parts actually playing that clip, and meta-layer sampling answers from the first part that has the data. It reaches the parts through the new `IArbitratedView` capability, so the root claim's PRIORITY is fanned down and each part claims through its **own** `AnimationArbiter` — playing the parts' views directly would go around the per-part arbiters that each part's `MotionPoseAnimator` claims on, and the walk cycle would take the part back on its next direction change. `CompositeZonedPlayer` now gives every part an arbiter unconditionally (not just parts with an authored `MotionPose`) and exposes its parts in authored order. Per-part *targeting* — "this state speaks for the legs only" — is deliberately NOT built here; the fan-out already selects a subset, so it is a parameter for `ZOE_PALETTE_BUILD_PLAN.md` task 6, not a redesign.
- **A revived character that had played a death clip was animation-locked forever (T-0094 follow-up; this is what the T-0091 entry above claimed but did not deliver).** `ReactionFxPlayer`'s end-of-reaction path cleared `_claimOwner` for every outcome, but a death deliberately does not hand the arbiter back (`handsBack` is false) — so the release never happened there, and `OnRevived`'s `if (_claimOwner != null)` then found nothing to release either. The arbiter kept a dead token at priority 1000 with no expiry, which nothing can outrank: after a revive, locomotion was refused forever (the body frozen on the death clip's last frame), a new hurt was refused, and even a second death was refused (1000 is not strictly greater than 1000), so the character could never visibly die again. `_claimOwner` now keeps pointing at a death's claim exactly as its own doc comment always promised, and `OnEnable` additionally hands back any claim carried over from a previous life, for a pooled corpse switched off and on again without ever being revived.
- **A refused newcomer still destroyed the reaction in flight when the VIEW, not the priority, was what refused it (T-0094 follow-up).** `ReactionFxPlayer.TryArmClip` asked the arbiter whether the claim would be accepted before tearing anything down, but nothing asked whether the view even knew the clip NAME — that only came out of `PlayClip`'s return value, several steps later, after the incumbent had already been retired: its unfired `OnFrame` effects flushed, its requester told it was cut short. So a misspelled, renamed or deleted clip name killed a perfectly good in-flight reaction and then played nothing at all. New `IAnimatedView.HasClip(clip)` (mirroring `ZonedAnimationPlayer.Play`'s own success condition exactly, so a yes here means a yes there) is asked first, alongside the priority test and before any teardown. It is a separate question from `GetClipSeconds`, which cannot answer it: that returns 0 for an unknown name AND for a valid zoned strip.
- **`MotionPoseAnimator` ignored the arbiter's answer, and wrote to the player through a second channel it never saw (T-0094 follow-up).** It discarded `AnimationArbiter.Play`'s return value while still recording the clip as started, so a refused claim still convinced it that it owned the body. Worse, `TryEnterZone`/`TryEnterFrame` went straight at the player every `LateUpdate`, unconditionally, regardless of what the arbiter had said — so a character whose `hitStun` is shorter than its hurt clip had the frame write yank it back onto the locomotion pose every frame the instant the stun ended, stomping the hurt animation through a door the arbiter could not see. Both now obey it: a refused claim yields, and zone/frame entry is gated on actually holding the claim. Yielding forgets the clip, zone AND held frame together, so the pose is fully re-stated when the body comes back rather than being deduped away as "already handled".
- **Claimants still writing clips straight to the view, so "everything routes through the arbiter" was not true (T-0094 follow-up).** `TargetPracticeController.PlayIdle` called `view.PlayClip` directly — and now that a death correctly interrupts a hurt, that call is reached from *inside* the death's own arming, pushing Idle over a death clip that was still being set up; it only ever looked right because the death happened to overwrite it a fraction of a millisecond later. It now claims at `PriorityLocomotion` and hands the claim straight back (holding it would starve a real locomotion/motion-pose animator at the same rung, since equal priority never steals and this component's `Start` runs first), and it no longer returns to Idle when the hurt was *interrupted* — something else has the body and is about to show its own thing. `PlayLauminationEffect`, an effect card with no priority at all, now claims at `PriorityNamedState`, with a non-looping clip auto-expiring after its own length so it can never paint over a hurt or a death and can never leave an anonymous claim nobody is left to release. `ReactionFxPlayer`'s second and later passes of a multi-loop reaction went to the view directly too (they cannot re-`Play`, which would hit the "same owner, same clip — don't restart" rule) and now go through a new `AnimationArbiter.Replay`, without which pass 2 of a looping reaction would never reach a composite body's parts. `MirageSubject`'s clip-sequence engine is deliberately left alone, with the reasoning written down at the call site: it drives the raw root player of a single-body preview subject and is skipped entirely in Target Practice mode, the one mode where real reactions play.
- **The three "your reaction did not play" signals disagreed for the identical condition (T-0094 follow-up).** For a refused clip, hurt fired `HurtFinished(true)`, death fired `DeathFinished(`**`false`**`)` — telling a listener a refused death had played out normally — and a named event fired neither, only the separate `EventRefused`, so a consumer awaiting `EventFinished` for a refused event hung unless it also happened to subscribe to the other one. There is now ONE stated contract, written in one place in `ReactionFxPlayer`, obeyed identically by all three: the signal fires exactly once for every reaction raised, and its bool always means "this did not reach its own end" — played out → `false`, cut short → `true`, refused → `true`, nothing to play → `false`. `EventRefused` stays, because it carries a fact the bool cannot: *why* nothing played.
- **`ZoeState` cached its `ReactionFxPlayer` at `Awake`, so one added later was never heard from (T-0094 follow-up).** `ReactionFxPlayer` and `LocomotionAnimator` both resolve lazily with explicit comments about this exact spawn-ordering trap; this one did not. `MirageSubject.RebindToExisting` → `EnsureTargetPractice` adds a `ReactionFxPlayer` to a hand-placed GameObject that may already carry a `ZoeState`, and the cached null meant `DeathFinished` was never subscribed and `DeathClipArmed` never consulted — a body that either vanished mid-death-animation or never vanished at all. Resolved lazily now, with the subscription retried on death itself so a late arrival is still caught.
- **`DeathDisposal.AfterDelay` started its linger at the moment of death instead of when the death animation ended (T-0094 follow-up).** The enum's own definition is "play the death clip, THEN lie there for `deathLinger` seconds", but the timer was scheduled from `OnDied`, so a 2 s death animation with a 1.5 s linger disposed the body half a second before its own animation finished — an overlap, not a linger. `AfterDelay` now waits on the same `DeathClipArmed`/`DeathFinished` handshake `WhenDeathClipEnds` uses (landing on T-0090's `_awaitingDeathClip` rather than fighting it) and starts the timer when the clip actually ends; with no clip playing there is nothing to wait for and the linger starts at once, exactly as before.
- **ZUI Envelope — the point floor was 0 (IMGUI) or a hardcoded 2 (UI Toolkit), so a curve could be deleted out from under itself or got stuck refusing to go below 2 points (T-0072).** The IMGUI `ZUI.Envelope` (`Zui/Scripts/Editor/ZUIEnvelope.cs`) had no minimum at all — double-click-to-remove and the Delete key could empty a curve to 0 points, which `ZUIEnvelopeEvaluator` only tolerated via a fallback value. It now refuses to remove the last remaining point at both sites, so 1 is the real floor; the UI Toolkit `ZuiEnvelope`'s `minPoints` default drops from 2 to 1 to match (every current caller uses the default, so this is the new system-wide floor, not a per-caller opt-in). At 1 point the curve is already correctly flat at that point's value end-to-end (`ZUIEnvelopeEvaluator.Evaluate` ignores time and returns `points[0].value`) and the point is fully draggable anywhere in the domain — `anchorsLocked` no longer force-locks it, since that rule exists to pin the two ENDS of a multi-point curve and a 1-point curve has no separate ends. The IMGUI control also gained a click-to-add band on the flat single-point line (`HitLine` only recognises real 2+-point segments), so a curve collapsed to 1 point isn't stuck there — clicking near the line adds a second point back, exactly like every other segment.
- **SpriteFx — the Stacks preview never appeared to switch the effect OFF, so a loop read as one continuous full-blast.** The preview's single "Restart after" dial was labelled like a wait but actually HELD the fully-filtered life-1 frame for its duration, which the runtime never does: `SpriteFxFilter.Update` calls `Restore()` at the end of a pass and puts the original unfiltered sprite back. Duration 0.33 + Restart after 1.00 therefore ramped for a third of a second and then sat at full blast for a full second, forever. That dial is gone, replaced by a single preview-only **Idle gap (s)** — show the plain sprite with the stack bypassed before looping, which is what actually ships. `RenderPreviewAt` gained a `bypassStack` flag that skips `SpriteFxStack.RunStack` / `SpriteFxFilter.Apply` and takes the unpadded path. The gap stays preview-only and is never written into the SpriteFx Stack asset.
- **ZoetropeLaunimator — a composite Zoe had NO draw order, so it could render inside-out everywhere except the one demo that patched it.** Composite parts share a position, so with every part's renderer left at `sortingOrder` 0 the front-to-back order was a tie the engine broke arbitrarily — legs over torso. Nothing in the composite data expressed draw order at all; the only place it was ever set was `ProtoGuySpawner` reaching in after spawn to bump the torso. That made the demo scene look correct while the REAL spawn path — a game, or Mirage — drew the character wrong, with nothing to reveal the difference. New `ZoeBodyPart.sortingOrder` (higher draws in front), applied by `CompositeZonedPlayer.Build`, exposed as "Draw order" in the Zoe window's Rig section, and set on ProtoGuy (Legs 0, Upper 1). The demo's patch is deleted along with its `upperSortingOrder` field: a demo that fixes something up locally is a demo that hides a missing feature. Found only because Mirage previews through the real spawn path and disagreed with the demo.
- **Zoetrope — a painted muzzle layer was never picked up, because the tracker choice was made from the SCREEN instead of the data.** `ZoeSpawner.EquipWeaponSlots` decided between `MuzzleVectorTracker` and `MuzzleTracker` by asking the live view whether the layer resolved *at that instant* — but it runs inside `Start()`, before any clip has been resolved, so it saw whatever animation loaded first (for a composite Zoe, the version's first) and concluded no muzzle layer existed. It then attached **no tracker at all**, permanently, and the muzzle stayed pinned to the weapon's fixed `muzzleOffset` no matter what was painted (observed on a live ProtoGuy: weapon at x −1.51, muzzle at x −1.01 — exactly the `(0.5, 0)` offset). New `IAnimatedView.GetMetaLayerKind(layerId)` + `MetaLayerKind {None, Point, Vector}` answer from the version's own animations, so the decision is order-independent; `AnimatedViewRelay` implements it by scanning `version.animations` (Vector wins over Point when both are declared — it carries strictly more information). Verified against a throwaway in-memory version: while playing a clip WITHOUT the layer, the old screen-sample returns false and the new lookup correctly returns `Vector`.
- **Laumination Builder — the Registration preview drew EVERY frame superimposed whenever nothing was selected, and the Ghost counts couldn't turn it off.** The onion-skin's window check was gated on having an anchor (`if (anchor >= 0 && …)`), and the anchor is the selected sprite's slot — so with no selection the check was skipped entirely and all N frames drew at once, no matter what Ghosts `<`/`>` were set to. On a 3-frame walk cycle that fallback was harmless; on a 16-direction rotation sheet it stacked 16 poses at 0.28 alpha into an unreadable smear that overflowed the stage — and since nothing is selected when the window OPENS, that was the first thing you saw for every rotation-sheet asset. Now: ghost counts of 0/0 mean off unconditionally, the onion-skin centres on the PLAYHEAD when there's no selection instead of falling back to "ghost everything", and with nothing selected the preview draws the current frame solid (registration is judged against one frame on the crosshair, so that's the useful default). Reported from a screenshot; the diagnosis was confirmed against the live window's own `_ghostBefore=0 _ghostAfter=0`, `HasSelectedCell=False`, `AnchorSequenceIndex=-1` before anything was changed.
- **Laumination Builder — a Vector meta-layer had no Zoom or Center control at all.** `BuildVectorLayerUI` returned early, above the zoom slider / recentre button that the Shape and Point paths get, so placing a muzzle origin — pixel-precise work on a sprite tens of pixels across — was stuck at the default 6× with no way to magnify, and a stray middle-drag pan left the sprite off-view with nothing to bring it back. The row is now `BuildMetaViewRow` and every layer mode calls it. Found by walking the real ProtoGuy muzzle-painting task rather than by probing.
- **Laumination Builder — turning on Meta layers now pauses the preview.** The clip loops by default, so entering paint mode left the sprite cycling at 12 fps while the panel's own hint said "click the preview to place the origin" — a click then authored whatever frame happened to be up at that instant, silently landing on the wrong direction. Painting is per-frame, so paint mode now pauses on entry; the ▶/❚❚ button and clicking any strip frame (which already paused) are unchanged.
- **ZUI — `ZuiValueControl` no longer draws 6 px wider and ~5 px taller than a plain `MicroSlider` row:** the body inside its header row was still carrying the `zui-row` child gap after it and its own bottom margin under a control that already has one, so a value control packed beside plain sliders (a form card, a modifier card) sat out of grid, shifted the next column and opened the row spacing. Found while converting the Kiln forms' dials to envelopes.
- **ZUI — a reflected enum's radio row now wraps inside the pane** instead of running off its right edge and summoning a horizontal scrollbar: `ZuiReflect` lets the enum's field row shrink (`flexShrink 1`), which is what the wrapped `Z.MiniRadio` inside it needs to see a narrower container (found by the Orb's five-option Variant row; every reflected enum benefits).
- **Pyre — a Swarm's Area+Distribution-0 fill on a polygon shape (Triangle/Square/Pentagon/Hexagon) could read as an arbitrary, unevenly-spaced cluster instead of the shape it was supposed to fill (T-0070).** Two earlier attempts under this same Unreleased entry (both superseded here) shared one flaw: a single generic construction for every polygon kind — first a row-by-row raster grid clipped to the shape (settled on a small off-centre cluster at low `n`), then one concentric-ring "dartboard" scaled to the polygon's own boundary (fixed the low-`n` case, but scaling one ring's constant radial step by a boundary distance that swings sharply with angle does not hold equal-area bands, which read as visibly uneven clumping on a TRIANGLE specifically — reported at 27 particles, Distribution 0). Replaced with a DEDICATED layout per polygon kind, in `GridLayoutPolygon`'s new dispatch (`Runtime/Pyre/PyreRenderer.cs`): Triangle gets a true triangular lattice (the "bowling pin rack" — rows of 1, 2, 3… points, apex to base); Square gets an axis-aligned grid rotated into the shape's own diamond orientation; Hexagon gets a true hex/triangular lattice (the hexagon's own natural, densest tiling); Pentagon — no regular polygon tiling exists for it — falls back to the ring idea redone with equal-AREA bands (radius ∝ √k, not linear) and perimeter-uniform spacing (walking each ring by actual outline progress, not by angle) instead of the constant-count, angle-uniform version that caused the unevenness. `n == sides` lands every particle exactly on the shape's own (scaled) vertices for all four kinds. Verified per shape at three particle counts each (n = sides, 27, 50): rendered against the true outline and checked both by eye and by each layout's nearest-neighbour-distance spread (coefficient of variation 0.04–0.19, all tight), plus confirmed live on the actual reported Triangle asset ("Green Lantern") through the tool's own thumbnail renderer.

### Changed
- **PyrePlus — shapes are now plug-ins (`PlusForm`), and the first two live in their own assembly.** A layer can hold a `[SerializeReference] PlusForm form` (`Runtime/PyrePlus/PlusForm.cs`) instead of a `ShapeForm` enum case: the form is a self-contained class whose public fields ARE its dials (`[Range]`/`[Tooltip]`/`[PlusSwarmOnly]`/`[ZUIShowIf]` drive the editor through `ZuiReflect.FlowFields` — a form needs zero editor code), discovered by assembly scan (`[PlusFormInfo(name, group, icon)]` places it in the Shape picker, which now shows one column per group beside 3D / 2D / Special), evaluated through the renderer's one deterministic Eval funnel with auto-derived field ids (`FldForm - slot`), rendered by ONE dispatch at the top of `RenderLayer` that hands over the fill, alpha, prepared geometry/pixel modifiers and the full swarm-instance list (position with live spin/scale, own life, spawn life, index, orientation, depth, size/brightness), deep-copied and content-hashed by reflection. **Inferno and Fork Blast are migrated** into `InfernoForm` / `ForkBlastForm` in the new family assembly `com.Lautaro-Arino.Laubrary.PyrePlus.Forms.Kiln` (`Runtime/PyrePlus/Forms/Kiln/`, which the core never references — the template for every future family), verified byte-identical against the enum path they replaced (0 differing pixels over 12 frames, swarm off and on, defaults and ~35 tweaked dials each) before that path was deleted. Their ~80 `inferno*`/`fork*` fields, hand-written `Clone()` lines, `Fld*` ids, renderer branches and the two hand-written editor boxes are gone from the core; the two enum slots stay (`[Obsolete]`) so every other serialized int keeps its meaning, and a layer still carrying one without a form draws as a Disc. The two project assets that used Fork Blast were pointed at a `ForkBlastForm` carrying their authored values (0 px difference). Design + the "add a form = one file" recipe: `PYREPLUS_DESIGN.md` § Plug-in forms.

### Added
- **PyrePlus — Arc Burst renders through a Burst rasteriser; Lichten 356 → 77 ms per frame at 128 px (T-0062).** Step one, bit-exact and managed: `ArcField.Seg` skips any pixel whose energy and alpha planes already sit at or above the most the stroke could deposit there (the composite is a pure maximum; the bound comes from monotone tables widened by 1e-5), and `Blob` scans only the bbox of its largest radius — every layout's bytes unchanged, every layout ~2× faster. Step two: `ArcField` became a collector of blittable stroke / blob records and `ArcRaster.Run` (`Forms/Kiln/ArcRaster.cs`, `[BurstCompile]` Strict / High / CompileSynchronously, reached by direct call on the calling thread so worker-thread fills need no job handle) deposits them with maximum compositing through `fixed` pointers over the existing planes — the only path, no managed twin, because Burst's exp/pow differ from Mono's by a last bit. The Kiln family asmdef references `Unity.Burst` / `Unity.Collections` / `Unity.Mathematics` and allows unsafe code; the PyrePlus core does not (legacy Disc FNV unchanged). Parity: all ten contract draws PASS every stage (IoU 1.000, field MAD ≤ 1.5e-6, pixel rgb MAD ≤ 0.083); Bolt / Terminal / Stipple / Pinch byte-identical to before, the other six moved by a last bit. Per-layout table, serial-vs-parallel identity and the recipe in `PYREPLUS_DESIGN.md` § "Burst-boosting a form". With Burst compilation disabled in the editor the same source runs under Mono (correct, slower) and a one-time warning says so.
- **PyrePlus — the preview cache is per LAYER: hide/show/reorder and spec-level post edits only recomposite; a dial on one layer re-renders that layer alone (T-0061).** The frame cache stored whole composited frames, so any edit — including toggling a layer's visibility — threw every frame away and re-rendered the whole stack (Lichten + Plasma Bloom + Disc swarm + Inferno at 128 px × 24: 1.8 s for every toggle). Each layer's rendered buffer is now cached on its own under a content key (`PlusLayerKey`: the layer's serialized fields, its index, canvas/frames/seed, the global geometry/pixel modifiers) in a 64 MB LRU store (`PlusLayerCache`), and frames are composited from the cached buffers (`PyrePlusRenderer.ComposeFrame`) — mattes, clips, deferred borders and the spec-level posts applied live. Measured: hide or show a layer 0 renders (25–60 ms), edit a Disc dial 24 layer-frames (36 ms), edit a Lichten dial 24 layer-frames (the heavy layer is the whole cost), swap two adjacent layers 48 (the renderer's layer salt is the index), undo / redo of any of these 0 renders, add a global Bloom 0 renders. Render and compose jobs run on worker threads (`PlusLayerFill`, same clone/cancel discipline as T-0060); the frame on screen still composites synchronously first; the `rendering n/N` readout counts frames and its tooltip says how many layer-frames the fill needs, found cached and rendered. The renderer was factored into one per-layer path (`PyrePlusRenderer.Layers.cs`: `PlanFrame` / `RenderLayerBody` / `FrameComposer`) that `RenderFrame` itself runs through — its output is unchanged (bake, GIF, parity untouched). The cached composite equals `RenderFrame` exactly for every layer the renderer isolates and wherever a plain fast-path layer lands on transparent pixels; over earlier content such a layer differs by byte quantisation only (premultiplied colour and alpha within two steps). Seven new tests: exact composite for a seven-layer matte/clip/luma/window/hidden/border spec with global modifiers, the fast-path exact-and-bounded cases, the background-fused first layer, the key rules (one edit → one key; visibility → none; a move → the moved indices), the LRU store, worker-thread layer jobs. Design: `PYREPLUS_DESIGN.md` § "Per-layer preview cache".
- **PyrePlus — the preview cache fills on every core, byte-identical (T-0060).** The frame cache from T-0059 rendered one frame per editor tick on the main thread (Arc Burst / Lichten 128 px × 24 frames: 10.3 s). Frames are independent by the renderer's contract, so `PlusFrameFill` (`Runtime/PyrePlus/PlusFrameFill.cs`) now renders them on `ProcessorCount - 1` dedicated worker threads — one deep clone of the spec per worker, results drained and uploaded from the editor tick — and the same clip fills in ~1.1–1.5 s (renderer-only: 8.7 s → 0.93 s on 11 workers; Plasma Bloom 2.7 s → 0.21 s). Output is identical to the serial path: a new test renders a five-layer spec (Disc + Plasma Bloom + Arc Burst with swarm + ForkBlast under geometry/pixel/post modifiers + Inferno) serially and in parallel and asserts 0 differing pixels over nine runs and three worker counts, plus cold-vs-warm; the live cache textures were checked against `RenderFrame` (0 px) too. Made legal by fixing the renderer at the source: `_layerSalt`, `_textReady` and the swarm scratch lists are `[ThreadStatic]`; `PlusPrepassCache` is thread-safe and shared between a form and its render clones (`PlusForm.SharePrepassWith` / `PrepassIdentity`), so a Plasma fit is solved once, not once per worker; `PyrePlusRenderer.IsParallelSafe` names the layers that must stay serial (Fire / Fireball / simulation modifier / Text / Sprite / sprite-textured fills / Playback3D) and such specs keep the serial fill, as do specs under 100 ms of work (a 64 px Disc clip is not slower). The main thread only renders the frame on screen: measured tick gaps during a Lichten fill averaged 55 ms (idle baseline 39 ms) with a single 410 ms gap for that first frame, where the serial fill blocked ~360 ms on every tick. The transport's `rendering n/N` readout is unchanged; its tooltip now says which path the fill took and, for the last complete fill, wall time vs rendering time and thread count. Edits mid-fill cancel the workers (an in-flight frame finishes and is discarded; no new fan-out starts until they have exited, so a dial drag cannot pile up threads). GIF export untouched (serial). Also fixed on the way: the window's tick guards against the one extra call `EditorApplication.update` makes after a window closed from inside another update callback (a destroyed window used to allocate a fresh cache — and now a fill — that nothing would ever dispose). `PYREPLUS_DESIGN.md` gains the threading contract and the form recipe the re-entrancy rule.
- **PyrePlus — the preview renders each frame once and plays from a cache (T-0059).** The animated preview used to call the renderer every time the frame index changed, so looping playback re-rendered the whole clip on every loop and the editor froze between frames on a heavy form (Arc Burst / Lichten at 128 px × 24 frames: 358 ms per frame, 8.6 s per loop, every loop). New `Editor/PyrePlus/PyrePlusWindow.FrameCache.cs`: ONE frame cache shared by the animated preview and the Strip view (the old `stripCache` is gone), one RGBA32 point-filtered texture per frame allocated once per frameCount × canvas and re-rendered in place on an edit. The frame on screen renders immediately (a scrub or dial drag stays as responsive as before); the rest fill from the editor tick under a 12 ms budget (at least one frame per tick), forward from the current frame, repainting as they land; playback only steps onto cached frames, so the first loop waits at the render front instead of stalling the editor and every loop after it makes zero renderer calls (measured: 24 renders for the fill, 0 across three subsequent loops; fill 10.3 s wall for the 8.6 s of rendering). Any authored edit (`MarkDirty`) restarts the fill from the frame on screen; undo/redo and asset switches now invalidate too (`OnBeforeRebuild` — before, a paused preview kept the pre-undo frame). Layout/playback-only edits no longer invalidate: Frame/Strip toggles, zoom, speed, loop delay and every CherryFraming sequence edit go through `DirtyRepaintOnly`. A subtle `rendering n/N` readout sits at the end of the transport row while the fill runs (reserved width, hidden when done, never reflows); its tooltip carries the last complete fill's wall time and slowest frame. Renderer untouched; GIF export still renders through the renderer, byte-identical.
- **PyrePlus — geometry modifiers now apply to every plug-in form (runbook step 0, T-0058).** Wobble / Rotate / Scale / Skew / Turbulence and the rest of the `GeometryModifier` family did nothing on Plasma Bloom, Arc Burst and ForkBlast (only Inferno warped per sample). New `Runtime/PyrePlus/PlusFormWarp.cs`: after a form renders its isolated scratch, `RenderFormLayer` builds one nearest-sample inverse map of the layer's geometry stack (Inferno's conventions — canvas-centred pixel offsets, `GeoCtx` radius `min(W,H)/2`, highest `WarpPass` first, the renderer's per-frame phase) and resamples the finished buffer through it; published parity planes go through the same map so dumps stay aligned. `PlusForm.HandlesGeometry` (virtual, false) lets a form that warps per sample opt out — `InfernoForm` does, so it is not warped twice. Layer-centre semantics for swarms (the whole layer buffer warps once; a per-instance warp is a possible later refinement, not built). The pass is skipped when the layer has no geometry modifiers: Disc, Plasma Bloom, Arc Burst, ForkBlast, Inferno and Inferno + Scale 0.5 are byte-identical to 41fbacfe (64 px × 8 frames). Tests: 5 new in `Assets/Tests/PyrePlus/PlusFormWarpTests.cs` (identity stack byte-identical, Rotate 90° an exact quarter-turn permutation directly and through the renderer on all three ported forms, Wobble preserves coverage, Inferno not double-warped) — 101/101. Design: `PYREPLUS_DESIGN.md` § Modifier support for forms.
- **PyrePlus — `ArcBurstForm`, Port 02 of the Kiln catalogue: the Energy Explosion "electric arc burst" (agent4, GENERATION 4) as a PlusForm, exact against all ten published draws.** `Runtime/PyrePlus/Forms/Kiln/ArcBurstForm.cs` (dials) + `PlusArcBurst.cs` (algorithm) + `PlusPyRandom.cs` in the Kiln family assembly, picker group "Kiln/Energy Explosion". The source's ten draws are ten programs over one stroke engine, so they are ten `layout`s of one form (Core, Weave, Bolt, Crown, Lattice, Terminal, Cage, Stipple, Pinch, Lichten — a wrapped mini-radio), each ported with its own literals: the shared dials (alpha normalisation `aref`/`agamma`, bloom radius/strength and the alpha share, width/amp scales, `keepHueFloor`, deep-ghost range, `rerollPerFrame`, the five cel `bands` as a PlusRamp step table) plus one `[ZUIShowIf]`-gated settings box per layout holding that draw's counts, reach/branch ranges, ghost probabilities, dissolve/cool starts and its signature event (the bolt's travelling blow-out, the weave's shutter, the cage's back-face opacity, the lichten's hollowing front …), every default the draw_* literal. Every component is ported — Gaussian-profile strokes deposited with MAXIMUM compositing into an energy plane and an already-normalised alpha plane, core+sheath channels, filled irregular bodies with periodic radius profiles, midpoint-displacement filaments, recursive bolt trees with bounded-random branch counts re-rolled per frame (the source's look; `rerollPerFrame` off holds the stream so a bolt grows), multi-scale jitter, travelling / random / radial opacity veils, the 3-pass zero-padded box bloom (alpha at 0.34× over 0.8× the radius), angular and radial alpha fades, the energy hole, and the 5-band cel `colorize` (colour by threshold, alpha = the A plane truncated to a byte, absent under the lowest band). `PlusPyRandom` replicates CPython's `random.Random` (MT19937 + init_by_array, `random`, `getrandbits`, `randrange`/`randint`/`choice` rejection sampling, `uniform`, `gauss` with its cached second value) bit-for-bit, so the form draws the contract's OWN bolts: the parity harness passes every stage on all 10 draws (field Pearson ≥ 0.99999999998, silhouette IoU 1.000, exposure 0, ramp ΔE 0, pixel MAD ≤ 0.035). Lengths are the source's 128 px frame scaled to the canvas; a canvas under 128 renders k× supersampled and box-downsamples premultiplied so sub-pixel filaments survive at 64 px. Ten `ArcBands.*` palettes (the agent's `PAL`). Swarm: one burst per particle at its position and life; `IPlusFieldPublisher` (`H`, `ramp_t`, `alpha`) + `IPlusRampProbe` (banded, quantised to the contract's 256-entry LUT grid). No core edits — the default path is byte-identical by construction (Disc 48 px × 12 frames hashed identical to the Port 01 baseline). Tests: 11 new in `Assets/Tests/PyrePlus/ArcBurstTests.cs` (96/96). Kiln's `parity_compare.py` fixed so the log-histogram EMD anchors at 1e-4 of the peak instead of the smallest positive float (it was scoring float32-cumsum noise in 1e-25 exp tails as EMD 0.13 on planes identical to 1e-5).
- **PyrePlus — `PlasmaBloomForm`, Port 01 of the Kiln catalogue: the Energy Explosion "plasma bloom" (agent3_fork, GENERATION 5) as a PlusForm, byte-exact against all ten published draws.** `Runtime/PyrePlus/Forms/Kiln/PlasmaBloomForm.cs` (dials) + `PlusPlasmaBloom.cs` (algorithm) in the Kiln family assembly, picker group "Kiln/Energy Explosion". Every source component is ported — Gaussian shell with cosine bias (harmonic K), fbm front warp, cos/noise lobes and the Plume tongue shell, the soft half-plane gate, drift with the per-piece wake lag, the body→chunks fracture crossfade, polar seamless turbulence, additive embers and motes, core flash, remnant, beaded ghost ring, the two-power dissipation gain, the dual-ramp shade keyed to the travelling front, and the 2× premultiplied supersample — and the source's `_place`/`_fit` measurement solve (four-direction visible extent against the room per direction, then a border-cap shrink loop) runs as a cached pre-pass (`PlusPrepassCache<PlasmaFit>`, `autoFit`) so the blast is always placed and sized by its own field and a dial edit cannot clip it; `autoFit` off exposes hand `scale`/`orgX`/`orgY`. Dial names are the contract's parameter keys (`expRate`, `w0`, `coreGain`, …; the three piece populations are nested `PlasmaPopulation` boxes) with the meaning in every tooltip; defaults are draw `detonate`'s resolved values; lengths are fractions of the solved radius / canvas half-size so the form looks the same at 64 px as at the source's 128 (verified: the fit solves rMax = 0.789 of the half-size at both). `PlusNumpyRng` replicates numpy's `default_rng` (SeedSequence + PCG64, `random()`, `shuffle()`) bit-for-bit, so the populations and noise lattices are the source's OWN numbers — the parity harness passes every stage on all 10 draws (field Pearson 1.000, silhouette IoU 1.000, pixel MAD 0.0; the fit solves each draw's `rMax`/`orgX`/`orgY` to the contract's value). Swarm: one bloom per particle (its position is the source, its own life the blast clock); `IPlusFieldPublisher` (`H`, `ramp_t`, `alpha`, `rim_mix`) + `IPlusRampProbe`. Core additions (all additive, default path byte-identical — Disc 48 px × 12 frames hashed identical against a7c8a966): `PlusRampPresets.PlasmaIon/Cryo/Volt/Toxin/Flare` + `Plasma(name)` (the agent's five sRGB palettes, LUT-tested against the contract), a float-plane `PlusSupersample.Downsample` that quantises ONCE after the box mean (the byte-first overload drops specks under 0.5/255), and `PlusForm.UsesFill` (a form with its own ramps hides the layer Fill row instead of showing a dead control). Tests: 11 new in `Assets/Tests/PyrePlus/PlasmaBloomTests.cs` (85/85). Kiln's `parity_compare.py` fixed to compare silhouettes by the same mask KIND per frame (H>lo only where BOTH sides dumped H).
- **PyrePlus — shared form capabilities (wave 1) + the Kiln parity harness.** Six small libraries every `PlusForm` may call, all pure and opt-in so a layer that does not use them renders byte-identical (verified: legacy Disc + ForkBlastForm, swarm off/on, 0 differing bytes): `PlusFieldOps` (separable Gaussian / running-window box blur, N-tap and exponential IIR directional smear, bilinear domain warp, bloom, multiply-by-noise, plane combine ops, lit-pixel statistics with q50/q90/q99, tileable quintic value noise 2D/3D + fbm + curl); `PlusShade` (a LUT baked in LINEAR light with per-stop alpha from `PlusRamp`/stops/Gradient/ZuiFill, hard banded fills + Kiln's `steps` quantise, dual-ramp smoothstep crossfade and a 2-D palette grid, additive emissive `1−exp(−k·L)` tone map with white blow-out); `PlusRamp` (a serialisable ramp a form carries as a field; `PlusRampPresets.Ember()`/`EmberSoot()` ship the gen-7 Fork Blast ramps verbatim — a test checks the baked LUT against the contract's LUT within one 8-bit step); `PlusClipStats` (field statistics over the WHOLE clip, computed once through the real Eval funnel and cached — the documented cure for the per-frame auto-exposure that flattened the ForkBlast brightness arc); `PlusSupersample` (render at k× and box-filter down with premultiplied alpha); `PlusPrepassCache<T>` (a spec-hash-keyed one-shot pre-pass, the fire harness's invalidation without replay). `PyrePlusField`'s metaball sample/accumulate/threshold/relief helpers are public. The swarm hand-off to whole-layer forms now folds `swarmScaleByIndex` into `sizeMul` exactly as `RenderSwarm` does; `PlusFormCtx` gained `frameCount`, `LifeOfFrame`, `PrepareCtxAt(life)` and `WithSize`. `PlusForm.Clone()`/`ContentHash()` deep-copy / content-hash any nested plain `[Serializable]` class (a `PlusRamp`) instead of aliasing it. **Parity harness** (`Editor/PyrePlus/Parity/PlusParityDump.cs`, no window, no menu item): `FromContract(dir, formType)` builds a spec from a Kiln `params.json` (same-named params set by reflection, the rest listed in `meta.json`), `Dump(spec, dir)` writes lossless top-down RGBA PNGs + `.npy` float planes from forms implementing `IPlusFieldPublisher` (installed only for the dump's duration through `PlusFormDebug.FieldSink`) + an 11-point `ramp_probe.json` (forms may answer via `IPlusRampProbe`), `Compare` shells out to `D:/CODEZ/Kiln/parity_compare.py` and returns the verdict + report paths. `ForkBlastForm` publishes its `H`/`T` planes. Editor tests in `Assets/Tests/PyrePlus/` (`Laubrary.PyrePlus.Tests`). Design notes: `PYREPLUS_DESIGN.md` § Shared capabilities / § Parity harness.
- **ZUI — `ZuiReflect` draws nested plain `[Serializable]` class fields.** A reflected object's field whose type is a plain serialisable class (not a `UnityEngine.Object`, not a list) used to be silently skipped; it now renders as a keyed box flowing the nested object's fields (depth-guarded). First user: PyrePlus's `PlusRamp` on a form.

- **PyrePlus — `ShapeForm.Playback3D`, a PROOF-OF-CONCEPT form sourced from a real 3D animation instead of a procedural closed-form/sim shape.** A new whole-layer form (`playbackPrefab`/`playbackSpeed`/`playbackScale`/`playbackTint`/`playbackScrub01`/`playbackLoopDuration`/`playbackPixelated`/`playbackPixelGrid` on `PyrePlusLayer`) whose whole point is the EDITOR PREVIEW experience, not a bake: `PyrePlusPlayback3DPreview.cs` drives a `PreviewRenderUtility` scene that instantiates the assigned prefab (an object-reference picker — the "never type a reference string" rule — typically a `ParticleSystem`-based fire/explosion burst) and applies Speed/Scale/Tint straight to every `ParticleSystem.MainModule` on it (no reflection needed), then `Simulate`s to the Scrub position each render for exact, repeatable positions. A **Pixelated preview** toggle switches the preview from the live 3D render to a point-filtered downsample at a configurable grid size (default 32, `Z.Toggle`/`Z.Int`), scaled back up with `FilterMode.Point` so the pixelation is legible — a rough gauge of how a baked pixel-art version would read. When the selected layer is this form, `PyrePlusWindow.Preview.cs`'s preview island shows this live 3D/pixelated render INSTEAD OF the normal composited 2D canvas (filmstrip mode is skipped too) — this form has genuinely no bake path into that canvas yet. **Honest scope: there is NO runtime bake for this form** — `PyrePlusRenderer`'s `Playback3D` case is a documented no-op stub (the layer contributes no pixels), so a spec using this form does not yet render correctly outside the editor. A built-in stand-in prefab (`Assets/Demos/PyrePlusDemo/Playback3DStandIn.prefab`, two Shuriken `ParticleSystem`s tuned to a rough fire/explosion burst) exists only to prove the pipeline — the intended REAL content is a purchased 3D VFX asset the user still needs to import by hand (Asset Store packages cannot be imported headlessly in this environment).
- **PyrePlus — `ShapeForm.ForkBlast`, a swarm-of-puffs radial detonation (ported from a Kiln agent's own generator, "agent3_fork_explosive" / Flame generation 5, per `D:\CODEZ\Kiln\docs\pyreplus_briefing.md`'s porting checklist).** A new whole-layer form (`Runtime/PyrePlus/PlusForkBlast.cs`) built from a **field of hundreds of small anisotropic soft puffs** thrown outward from a source and shaped by drag/entrainment, union-accumulated into one heat field and shaded once — distinct from Inferno's smooth volumetric metaball cloud, this reads as a gritty, particulate blast. Closed-form (no sim, no accumulated state): a puff's position/size/amplitude is a pure function of its slot index, the blast's own clock and the layer's dials. **The port's whole point is generation 5's death model** — the source's own note was *"the whole blast becomes a bit too transparent... let the explosion die out by shrinking rather than fade"*: **Hold** pushes a puff's fade to the back of its life instead of dimming from birth, **Shrink**/**Shrink at** contract a puff rather than fading it (the blob kernel's peak is its amplitude, so a shrinking puff stays exactly as bright at its centre), **Lead die** kills the fastest/outermost gas first so the silhouette closes INWARD instead of leaving a widening necklace of lumps at the old size, and **Opacity** is an exponent on the Fill's own alpha ramp that solidifies the body while leaving the already-thin rim alone. **Gobs** (optional, 0 default) shed burning mass off the blast in its opening phase that dissipates by ballooning — the opposite death to the body's, which is what makes the lumps read as having come off something. **Placement is SWARM-NATIVE**, Inferno's exact precedent: swarm off = one centred detonation, swarm on = every spawn ignites its own blast. **Progress** (id −55) is a time-remap envelope like Inferno's; **Reach** (−56) and **Flash** (−57) are the other two animatable dials, everything else (Spread/Bias/Aim, Drag/Buoyancy/Growth/Swell/Elongation, the birth-timing Skew/Span, Cool, the whole death-model quintet, the Gob sub-group, puff/turbulence/heat-field-mapping dials) is a structural per-blast constant, mirroring the source's own `JetSpec` (none of its ~40 fields were envelopes either). **Named drops** (the porting checklist's "what has to be dropped" requirement, not glossed over): multi-blast arrays are replaced entirely by the Swarm; rings/burning-fragment-trails/sparks/the non-gob "shed" stream/swirl-spin-lobes are out of scope (generation 3/4 texture, not "how it dies"); the source's per-pixel POLAR domain warp is approximated by a cheap per-puff tangential wobble, not a faithful port; the source's independent second colour ramp ("soot") is approximated by desaturating the Fill's sampled colour toward a fixed grey rather than a true second-ramp crossfade; the always-on "root" ember is folded into Flash. Verified: compile-clean; rendered off-screen via reflection (single-blast, swarm multi-blast, gobs) — non-degenerate, exact frame-to-frame determinism confirmed by re-rendering the same frame twice; a baked PNG contact strip eyeballed showing bloom → solid opaque body → visible contraction → a couple of dissipating gob lumps trailing off, matching the source reference behavior.
- **Zoetrope — custom Zoe events get a real authoring surface, and a frame cue's "raise" is now a PICKER instead of free text.** `Zoe.events` (named reactions beyond the fixed Hit and Death) had a runtime model and no way to author it: the Zoe window's hand-curated body never drew the list at all, so the only way to declare one was the raw Inspector. It now lives **inside Reactions, directly under Hit and Death**, because a custom event is not a different kind of thing — each one is a folding card whose body is the *same* reaction editor those two get (clip picker, the effect list, the add-effect palette), and whose header carries the id that is its whole contract with everything that raises it. Add / remove / drag-reorder; a new event arrives with a unique starting id already filled in and the field focused, so naming it is typing, not hunting; the empty state says what the block is for and offers the one move out of it. **Both ways an id can silently fail are now flagged as you author, not at runtime:** an empty id (nothing can raise it, and `Zoe.EventIds` skips it) and a duplicate (`EventNamed` returns the FIRST, so every later twin is dead code) each raise an amber badge on the card that names the consequence, recomputed live across the whole list as you type — and the id field's tooltip states plainly that renaming does not update whatever already raises it. **The cue list stopped being a default reorderable-list PropertyField** and became the same kind of card: its raise-event control is a dropdown over the owning Zoe's own declared ids (fed by `Zoe.EventIds`, which exists for exactly this), and its two trigger names are dropdowns over the frame events and Point meta-layers the view's clips actually authored, gathered across every clip since a cue is not bound to one.
