- **ZUI fill — tooltips no longer say "the particle's life".** PyrePlus vocabulary in a control shared by TextSplash, Pyre and others; reworded to "the subject's life". The Centre tooltip now also states that under Uniform fit both axes are scaled by the box's longer side, which is why a small vertical offset moves a long way on a wide subject.
- **ZUI fill — a FIT dial on spatial fills, answering "why do I only ever see part of my gradient".** A Linear/Radial fill is normalized into ZuiFill's `-1..1` (u,v) by the consumer, and TextSplash used ONE divisor for both axes (the box's larger half-extent) so a Radial fill would stay a true circle. The unavoidable consequence was that the SHORT axis never reached ±1: measured on a real "SPLASH!" line (half-extents 19.84 × 3.54), a vertical Linear ramp only reached `t` 0.412–0.588 — **18% of the authored gradient**, with the other 82% unreachable. `ZuiFill.FillFit` now offers `Uniform` (the old behaviour, default, byte-identical) and `Stretch` (each axis divided by its own half-extent). Same line, same ramp, Stretch: `t` 0.008–0.992, **98%**. For Radial the difference is character rather than range — both span the full ramp, but Uniform on a wide box is a circle sampled as a thin horizontal slice, so it reads as vertical bands, where Stretch is an ellipse fitted to the box and actually reads as radial.
  - The arithmetic moved onto `ZuiFill.Normalize(p, origin, half)` so consumers stop rolling their own — which is precisely how the package ended up with two silent conventions (TextSplash divided by the larger half-extent, PyrePlus's background per axis) that no author could see or choose between.
  - The dial is **opt-in per tool** (`ZuiFillControl.Options.showFit`), on in TextSplash. A `fit` that a tool's sampling ignores would be a control that does nothing, which is worse than no control; PyrePlus's background is inherently full-box and its text path uses spatial `Evaluate` only for textures, so neither faces the choice.
- **TextSplash — a RASTER MODE axis: how a glyph becomes cells is now an authored choice (SDF / Coverage / Pixel font).** Pixelation had exactly one rasterizer, and at small cell counts it was the wrong one: TMP's distance field sampled once per cell leaves ~98% of covered cells partly transparent and turns a two-colour splash into 183 distinct ones, which is what the alpha cutoff and the palette lock spend their time undoing. The mode is orthogonal — it composes with every existing pixelation dial — and a `version 3` migration seeds the new oversample (`rasterMode` deliberately gets no migration, because its zero already means SDF, which is exactly what existing splashes render as).
  - **Coverage** renders the splash oversampled (x2/x4/x8) and reduces it to the pixel grid by a chain of exact bilinear halvings, so a cell's alpha is its TRUE area coverage rather than one point sample. Halving repeatedly rather than blitting straight down is the correctness argument: one blit from 8x samples only 4 of the 64 source texels a destination pixel covers, whereas each halving averages exactly 4 and feeds the next. Averaging the buffer as it stands is valid only because TMP writes PREMULTIPLIED colour. The reduction is a public static shared by the runtime rig and the editor preview so the two cannot drift.
  - **Pixel font** rasterizes the FACE at the sampling size the splash actually renders at (`fontSize / pixelSize`, so one font pixel lands on one cell) with `RASTER_HINTED` grid fitting, and is the only mode that produces true 1-bit output. Baked automatically into a committed project asset beside the splash, on the same unattended maintenance the border font already rides — no button.
- **TextSplash — a premultiplied bitmap shader ships in the package's own Resources.** TMP's `TextMeshPro/Mobile/Bitmap` breaks the rig's contract twice: it outputs straight colour under a `SrcAlpha OneMinusSrcAlpha` blend, so into a transparent-black buffer it stores alpha SQUARED — on a different scale from the RGB beside it and from everything downstream (`Crisp` un-premultiplies by that alpha, the palette lock matches the result, `fixEdgeAlpha` presents through a premultiplied blend) — and a perfectly binary mask HIDES the bug, because 0 and 1 are the fixed points of squaring. It is also stripped from player builds, being in no Resources folder and referenced by no shipped material. Living in the package fixes both.

### Fixed
- **TextSplash — a bevel can no longer be applied to a bitmap face.** `ApplyBevel` unconditionally assigned the SDF shader and enabled `BEVEL_ON`, which over a 1-bit atlas reads the mask as a distance field and lights the glyph from noise. Bevel and the dilated border (which needs a distance field to dilate) are both gated off in Pixel-font mode, and the window says so rather than letting the dials look live.
- **Mirage — every Pyre (and Sprite) edit now refreshes the preview INSTANTLY (combat batch #7).** A Pyre previewed in Mirage is a bare `BlastPlayer` that fetches its frames ONCE and never re-fetches; the invalidation bus only cleared the static frame cache, so an edit didn't show until the next Play/Stop or view switch — "you tweak a setting, it seems to do nothing, then it shows up later." `MirageRig` now subscribes to `AssetCacheInvalidation.Invalidated` and — deferred one tick, to stay out of Unity's reentrant ObjectChange dispatch (the "Access version should be odd" trap) — DESTROYS + re-realizes any live entry whose content is the edited asset, the same destroy+respawn approach `MirageSubject` already uses for a Zoe, done at the one `[ExecuteAlways]` choke point that runs in edit mode. It rides the general ObjectChangeEvents-fed bus, so it can't be forgotten by a future dial and covers edits made in the plain Inspector or via Undo too. (Zoe entries are skipped — their own `MirageSubject` already handles them.)
- **ZUI fill — the Linear-axis and Grid rotation angles are MicroSliders (UI-Guide compliance pass).** Both rotation-angle fields were scrub `Z.Float`s; converted to a shared `AngleSlider` = `Z.MicroSlider` (0..360°, whole degrees), per the bounded-scalar-belongs-in-the-track rule (the Grid angle moved to its own row so the slider isn't cramped beside the Space / Width scrubs).
- **Zoetrope Zoe-events editor — the effect card's enum pickers are radios/segmented, not a native dropdown (UI-Guide compliance pass).** Trigger / Position / Direction / Scalar used a UITK `DropdownField`, against the layout rule "enum → radios/segmented, NEVER a dropdown." `EnumDropdown` → `EnumPicker`: `Z.Segmented` (≤3 options) / a wrapping `Z.MiniRadio` (more). The Event / Layer pickers stay dropdowns — they're dynamic authored-name lists, which the rule explicitly allows. Verified: 0 native dropdowns in the card.
- **ZUI section — a collapsed section now shows a ▸ so it can always be reopened.** A folded `ZuiSection` kept only a dimmed (75 %-opacity) header and NO caret, so a collapsed section read as "gone" — a user folded one (the Zoe editor's Reactions) and couldn't find it to reopen. A ▸ now appears on the header ONLY while collapsed (open sections stay caret-free, as before), making "click to expand" unmistakable.
- **ZUI gradient Phase now scrolls a full SEAMLESS loop — range 0→2, not 0→1 (#2 follow-up).** Phase ran 0..1, which only reached the mirror's midpoint — there was no way to scroll all the way around. It now runs 0..2: 0→1 plays the ramp forward, 1→2 plays it back reversed, and 2 lands exactly where 0 did, so animating Phase over life (a rising Curve 0→2) loops with no jump. The runtime already mirrored via `PingPong`, so only the control's range widened — phase 0 is unchanged and byte-identity holds.
- **ZUI fill layout now matches the target mockup, and the Fill card's fold caret is removed (#2).** The Fill header lays out as the mockup drew it: row 1 = [preview square][Fill label][objective output ramp →][⋯], row 2 = the editable source ramp (full width, starting under the label), the square spanning BOTH rows on the left; and the fold ▾ caret is dropped — the header still folds the Adjust / Placement body on click, it just carries no glyph (`ZuiFoldCard.Wire` gains a `showCaret:false` overload). Verified by a layout probe: row 1 holds label | output | menu on one line, the source ramp sits below and wider, and the square spans both rows.
- **ZUI layout-rules compliance for the colour-recolour UI (`ZuiGradientControl`, the recolour inspectors).** Two real violations, both against `ui-layout-rules.md`: (1) `ZuiGradientControl`'s Hue / Saturation / Brightness / Contrast / Quantise rows used `Z.Slider` + a SEPARATE external value field (the named "pre-MicroSlider" anti-pattern) — switched to `Z.MicroSlider` (label + value inside the track), so every gradient editor (Pyre fills, the ColorRemap region tables, the gallery) now reads as the ZUI standard. (2) `SpriteFxRecolorEditor` / `ColorRemapGpuEditor` (custom `CreateInspectorGUI` roots) never called `Z.Attach(root)`, so the ZUI stylesheet was absent and every control rendered UNSTYLED — MicroSliders collapsed/overlapped, the fill's "Placement" divider mis-positioned; adding `Z.Attach(root)` fixes both (this rule is now codified in the layout guide). Also gave `ColorRemapGpu.speed` and `ColorRemapRegion.cycleSpeed` real `[Range(0,4)]`s so `ZuiReflect` renders them as sliders, not bare number fields.

### Added
- **ZUI shape recall is now INLINE in the menu — a "Recall shape" button that only opened another panel was improper UX.** A control labelled with an action ("Recall shape") must DO that action on press, not open a separate panel where you then pick — pressing it recalled nothing, so it read as broken. Fixed by bringing the choices to where the label is: the shape/step thumbnails now sit DIRECTLY in the value's right-click config menu under the **Shape** section (Built-in / User toggles + ＋ Save + a "Click a shape to apply it." hint + the thumbnail grid), so one click on a thumbnail applies it immediately and closes the menu — no intermediate button. Implemented via `ZuiMenu.Custom((body, close) => …)` (the `close` action dismisses the menu on apply). The old `ZuiEnvelopePresetPopover` is renamed **`ZuiShapeBrowser`** with a `BuildBrowser(...)` element builder (it's no longer a standalone popover). The generators (Oscillation… / Cycles…) stay as menu items opening their own parameter popovers — those genuinely need their own space. This class of mismatch (action-label that just opens a chooser) is now codified in the UI-layout rules.
- **ZUI right-click config menu extended to `ZuiFill` and `ZuiBox` — and the redundant ⋯ button dropped.** Following the `ZuiValueControl` right-click work, the fill/texture menu on `ZuiFillControl` now opens on a **right-click anywhere on the control** (the ⋯ button is gone — the row tooltip hints "right-click to configure"), and right-clicking a `ZuiBox` title row toggles its gear panel (the ⚙ stays, since a toggle needs a visible on/off state). Every ⋯-style ZUI menu is now reachable by right-click, matching `ZuiSection`'s header menu.
- **ZUI Steps mode gains shape SAVE / RECALL (parity with Envelope shapes).** The shape browser (`ZuiShapeBrowser`) is mode-aware: on a **Steps** value it browses and saves STEP patterns instead of curves — the same one-click **＋ Save** + Built-in / User toggles, but with bar-graph thumbnails (a new `StepThumb`) and four built-in patterns (Rise / Fall / Bell / Alternate). `ZUIEnvelopePreset` carries an optional normalized `steps` list (a preset is a step preset exactly when that list is non-empty, so both kinds share one library asset and each browser shows only its own kind); `ZUIEnvelopePresetLibrary.AddSteps` saves one; applying it sizes the value via `SetStepCount` to the pattern's bar count and remaps [0,1]→yMin..yMax. The Steps menu's **Shape** section shows the patterns inline (click to apply). Verified in-editor via PrintWindow: the Steps menu shows the four built-in bar thumbnails + ＋ Save, and the sequencer renders an 8-bar pattern.
- **PyrePlus rotation fields opt into the Cycles generator.** The particle Turn / Tilt / Roll / Spin and the three Swarm-spin axes (turn / tilt / roll) are marked `cyclic` (`Val(..., cyclic: true)`), so their Envelope ⋯ menu now offers **Cycles…** — the snap-back sawtooth that reads as continuous rotation over the particle's life (N wraps across 0..1). Non-angle fields stay non-cyclic, so Cycles only surfaces where a wrap is meaningful.
- **ZUI value control — RIGHT-CLICK opens the ⋯ config menu.** Right-clicking anywhere on a `ZuiValueControl` opens the same mode / shape / copy-paste menu the ⋯ button does — the button is now a shortcut, not the only way in. Registered in the BUBBLE phase, so a child that owns its own right-click (a MicroSlider's display-options menu) still wins on itself; a right-click on the label / body / empty space bubbles up and opens the value menu. Mirrors `ZuiSection.SetHeaderMenu`'s existing right-click-on-header. (ZuiValueControl only for now; the other ⋯ menus — ZuiBox gear, ZuiFill — can get the same treatment.)
- **ZUI MultiCont — a Steps mode, envelope Shape generators (Oscillation, Cycles), and a redesigned shape browser.** `ZUIValue` gains a fourth mode, **Steps** — a step sequencer: the envelope splits into N held sections you click/drag to set (`ZuiStepSequencer`), evaluated as held values over the same Dur/Warmup/Loop timing as an Envelope (`EvaluateSteps` / `SetStepCount` / `EnsureStepsDefaults`; copy-paste + Undo included). The ⋯ mode picker is now **Static · Min-Max · Envelope · Steps** (Curve relabelled "Envelope"; Steps is a peer via `Options.allowSteps`, default on). Under Envelope, a new **Shape** menu section adds two point-pattern GENERATORS — they WRITE the curve's points, so the result stays hand-editable — each in a live-preview popover: **Oscillation** (a wave between start/end with N peaks + a smoothness slider, 0 hard-triangle → 1 rounded/sine, reusing the existing `smoothness`) and **Cycles** (a snap-back sawtooth for a WRAPPING value — a rotation/hue — that reads as continuous motion and turns Loop on; gated behind a per-field `cyclic` opt-in, `Options.WithCyclic()`). The shape browser (`ZuiShapeBrowser`, the UITK successor to the IMGUI `ZUIEnvelopePresetPopup` — a tracked TODO now closed) is a **thumbnail browser shown inline in the Shape menu section**: two filter toggles (Built-in / User), a **one-click ＋ Save** (no name prompt — a shape is identified by its thumbnail, not a name), and a wrapping grid of clickable curve thumbnails that apply on click (user ones deletable; the library asset is created only on Save). The generator popovers use `ZuiPopover` (inherit the ZUI stylesheet, no separate OS window). The Toolkit asmdef references `ZUI.Editor` (cycle-free) for the preset data. Verified in-editor: Steps held-value eval (0/4/8 across sections), Oscillate/Sawtooth point math, Steps copy-paste round-trip, the recall popover builds + styles correctly (8 built-ins + toggles + save). (Slice-1 followed user feedback: dropped the name-gated Save that blocked saving, dropped shape names, added the Built-in/User toggles.)
- **TextSplash — a real SDF border, TMP's bevel, extruded 3D letters, and pixel-art-grade pixelation (Slice 6).** Four capabilities, all driven by one enabling change. (1) **The border is a TRUE equidistant outline.** The previous approach scaled each glyph QUAD, which is structurally wrong in three ways the user could see: a counter gets NO border at all (the twin's hole is bigger than the face's, so `o e a B D 8 0` had no inner edge), thickness is proportional to distance from the glyph centroid (the crossbar of `H`/`A` got almost none), and side thickness scales with quad aspect (`i l !` got a third the side thickness of their top). It is now `_FaceDilate`, which offsets the SDF `bias` itself and is therefore a pure outward translation with no bite into the face. Deriving it from the shader: the 50 % contour is `c = 0.5 − weight`, the AA band `Δc = 1` spans `_GradientScale` texels, so `_FaceDilate = 2·bw·samplingPointSize / _GradientScale − _WeightNormal/4`, with `RATIOS_OFF` + `_ScaleRatioA = 1` pinned so TMP's auto-normaliser stops capping it and `_WeightBold = 0` so the bold term stops eating ~15 % of the padding budget. (2) **`SplashFontBaker`** makes that reachable at all: TMP's outline saturates at `atlasPadding ÷ (2 × samplingPointSize)` of an em — about **4 % of the font size** on a stock asset, ten times short of the tool's 0.5 em range — so the border pass gets its own font asset rasterised from the same font at much larger padding, mirroring face metrics, kerning and OpenType features so the two lay out identically (a drift there slides the border out from under its letters). (3) **Bevel** — TMP ships per-pixel relief lighting (`_Bevel`, `_BevelOffset/Width/Roundness/Clamp`, `_LightAngle`, specular, ambient, diffuse) that the default material cannot reach, because `LiberationSans SDF Material` points at `TMP_SDF-Mobile` and every Mobile variant strips shading; enabling it swaps to `TextMeshPro/Distance Field` + `BEVEL_ON`. It survives a per-letter spin, because the effect lives inside the SDF rather than in screen space; its honest limit is that the light angle is fixed in GLYPH space. (4) **`SplashDepth`** stacks N copies stepped along each letter's own local −Z **after** its spin, giving real extruded block letters — TMP has no extrusion of its own (glyph z is hard-coded 0 at four sites and the vestigial `isVolumetric` path has zero call sites). The sides carry their own `ZuiFill` plus `sideBrightness`/`sideSaturation` (a luminance-weighted lerp, not an HSV round-trip) and either ramp into the face with `darken` or take the side colour outright with `flatSides` — the flat form is what reads as a solid block rather than a soft shadow. Each layer paints its own fill first and the side shading grades over it, so a gradient face gets an extrusion OF that gradient. Depth needs a camera-space canvas (an overlay canvas discards z), and that canvas now borrows `Camera.main` rather than adding a second URP Base camera, which was visibly hijacking the screen clear. (5) **Pixelation became actual pixel art.** The grid alone leaves a gradient a smooth ramp and leaves TMP's antialiased edge as a whole visible pixel of mush, so `colorSteps` (palette banding) and the new `alphaCutoff` (every pixel fully on or fully off, un-premultiplying a promoted pixel so it does not come out dark) do the work the grid cannot, with `_Sharpness` pushed to maximum so the threshold gets a cleaner buffer. **Verified in play mode**, by inspecting live objects and by measurement, not by eye: the border twin runs on a freshly baked atlas at **padding 128** (the face's is 9) with `_GradientScale` 129 and `_ScaleRatioA` pinned to 1 on the non-Mobile shader; `ApplyCpuStage` collapses a synthetic 64-colour gradient to **4 colours** and leaves alpha as **only 0 or 255**; and a captured 1280×800 frame at pixel size 8 contains **0 of 16 000 blocks that are not a single flat colour**. **Build hardening**, because three of these worked in the editor and would have silently degraded in a player build: the premultiplied presentation shader and a `BEVEL_ON` preset material both ship inside the package's own `Resources` (a shader in no Resources folder, no Always-Included list and no material is stripped, and a `shader_feature` variant survives only if an included MATERIAL enables it); the padded border font can be baked at author time into a committed asset, because TMP nulls `sourceFontFile` on a Static font asset so a runtime bake can only find the font through the AssetDatabase; and that committed asset has TMP's `clearDynamicDataOnBuild` unticked, or `TMP_PreBuildProcessor` would wipe exactly the glyph data the bake exists to commit. **Known limits, stated rather than hidden:** `borderWidth.perLetter` is driven line-wide (`_FaceDilate` is a per-material float) and warns once instead of silently falling back to the broken quad-scaling; borrowing the scene camera means opaque geometry nearer than the canvas plane can occlude the splash and it composites before that camera's post-processing; and the auto-derived border padding is sized from the width dial's MAXIMUM rather than the value in use, so it over-reserves atlas.

### Fixed
- **TextSplash — the per-letter toggles now actually do something, and five other corrections from live use (Slice 5).** Everything here came out of the user driving Slice 4 and finding it wrong. (1) **The per-letter scope toggles did nothing at all.** Every per-letter path was gated behind one master `perLetter` bool, so ticking "per letter" on Size by itself could not engage anything. `TextSplash.PerLetter` is now a DERIVED property — true whenever a spawn stagger exists or any `SplashScalar` is scoped per letter — so a scope toggle works standalone. What remains of the old master is `perLetterMotion`, which only forces per-letter MOTION when there is no stagger. (2) **Stagger is one `spawnStagger` + `order` on the asset**, not a knob on each transition: a letter needs ONE unambiguous lifetime for its per-letter curves to run over, which two independent staggers cannot give it. `order` cascades LeftToRight / RightToLeft / **CentreOut** / **EdgesIn** / **Random**; symmetric orders deliberately give a mirrored PAIR the same turn (`CentreRank`), because ranking them one apart reads as a lopsided animation. The exit then got its own `exitStagger` + `exitOrder` behind an `exitSameAsEntrance` switch, so a line can arrive left-to-right and leave centre-out while each letter still has exactly one lifetime (`LetterStart` uses `RankIn`, `LetterEnd` uses `RankOut`). (3) **Min-Max scalars rolled every frame** — `ZUIValue.Evaluate` calls `Random.Range` on each call, so a random size strobed instead of being a random size. `SplashScalar.Evaluate(life, rollKey)` now rolls ONCE per play (or per letter, when the scalar is per-letter) off `Sfx.Hash01`, the package's frozen deterministic hash, so a roll is stable across the preview, the runtime and a reload — and a per-scalar SALT keeps a random size and a random alpha from drawing the same number and moving in lockstep. (4) **`slideDistance` was a meaningless multiple of the screen**; it is now 0..1 where **0 = starts fully outside the viewport edge** (measured against the text's real extents, so it is genuinely hidden) and **1 = starts at its resting place**, via the shared `SplashGeometry.SlideExtreme`. (5) **`fade` is gone from both transitions** — the `alpha` MultCont already owns opacity and two independent fades just fought. (6) **`ZuiFill.Mode.OverLife` rendered as a vertical gradient that never changed over time**, which is not what the mode means: it now samples one flat colour at the current life, per letter when scoped that way, so a staggered line becomes a travelling colour sweep for free. TMP's vertex gradient is no longer used anywhere in the tool. Also: **Radial/Linear fills are sampled PER VERTEX** in the [-1,1] space `ZuiFill` actually expects (its radial branch is `sqrt(u²+v²)`) rather than baked into a top-to-bottom gradient, with one shared divisor so a radial fill stays CIRCULAR instead of squashing to the glyph's aspect — and `FillSpace.Stamped`/`Fixed` map to one gradient per letter / one across the whole line. And the transport's **pause no longer rewinds to 0** — only a play resuming from the very end restarts. **Verified in-editor:** compiles clean; the schedule and ordering reproduce exact expected values (ranks `0..7` / `7..0` / `3 2 1 0 0 1 2 3` / `0 1 2 3 3 2 1 0` / a valid permutation; with 8 letters at 0.1 stagger `OutBase` 2.0 and `Total` 3.0; split cascade at exit 0.05 gives `Total` 2.65 with `rankIn` 0..7 against `rankOut` 7..0, and letter 0 correctly holds the LONGEST lifetime because it enters first and leaves last); per-letter lifetimes genuinely differ (0.652 vs 0.348 at t=1.5); `slideDistance` 0/0.5/1 yields −600/−300/0 against a 1080-tall frame and a 120-tall text; a Min-Max scalar returns the identical value at life 0 and life 0.7 (78.25) but differs per letter (108.35) and is line-constant when line-scoped (98.24), with size and alpha rolling unrelated values; and `ZuiAudit` reports **0 findings with all 11 boxes open**.
- **TextSplash — pixelation genuinely works now, in the preview as well as at runtime (Slice 5).** The user reported "pixelation doesn't seem to do anything", and it didn't: it ran ONLY at runtime while the authoring preview — the whole point of the tool — never showed it, and the rig itself had never been executed once. The preview now renders through `LiveScenePreview.Render(rect ÷ pixelSize)` and point-scales the result back, which is genuine low-resolution rasterization rather than a filter, and it shares the runtime's CPU stage through the new public static `SplashPixelRig.ApplyCpuStage` so the two cannot drift. Four real runtime bugs were found and fixed in the never-run rig: **a near/far of 0.05/100 clipped away spinning glyphs** (a whole-line spin swings vertices in Z by half the line's width — hundreds of units), the **first frame rendered at the default orthographic size 5** because `Init` never applied the canvas scale, **`Attach` bailed out when only a SpriteFx stack was set**, and **two concurrent splashes claimed the same layer** and rendered each other into their own buffers. The premultiplied-alpha edge fringe is fixed behind an optional `fixEdgeAlpha`: TMP's SDF shader blends `One OneMinusSrcAlpha` so the buffer holds premultiplied colour, and uGUI's default material multiplied by alpha a second time, compositing the antialiased rim as `C·a²`. The `Hidden/Laubrary/TextSplash/PremultipliedUI` shader ships inside the package's OWN `Resources` folder rather than relying on `Shader.Find`, because a shader in no Resources folder, no Always-Included list and no material is stripped from a player build — it would have worked in the editor and silently switched itself off in builds. **Verified in play mode** by inspecting the live objects: the splash canvas is `ScreenSpaceCamera` on `[TextSplash] Pixel Camera` (orthographic, near/far 0.05/6000), its target is a **160×100 Point-filtered buffer** — exactly 1280×800 ÷ 8 — holding 117 lit pixels at full alpha, presented by a `[TextSplash] Pixel Presenter` overlay canvas whose `RawImage` draws that 160×100 texture into a 1280×800 rect through the `Hidden/Laubrary/TextSplash/PremultipliedUI` material, and the shader loads from the package `Resources` so the build path holds. (Note: `FindObjectsByType` cannot see any of this — the splash host is `HideAndDontSave`, so `Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll` is required.)

### Added
- **TextSplash — two independent transitions, animatable (MultCont) scalars, a filled wide border, and real pixelation (Slice 4).** The authoring model is reshaped around four asks. (1) **Two transitions instead of one shared setting.** `inTransition` / `outTransition` are `SplashTransition`s carrying their OWN `duration`, `direction`, `slideDistance`, `ease` (+ `easeCurve`), `spinDegrees`, `spinAxis`, `scale`, `fade` and per-letter `stagger` — so an entrance and an exit can differ completely (fly in from the left with a spin, fade straight out in place). The old `slideInDuration`/`slideOutDuration`/`inFrom`/`outTo`/`ease`/`slideDistance`/`spinDegrees`/`spinAxis`/`letterStagger` fields are gone. (2) **Better easing**: Linear / SmoothStep / EaseIn / EaseOut / EaseInOut / **Back** (overshoot-and-settle) / **Elastic** / **Bounce**, plus **Custom**, which reveals the transition's own `ZUIValue` envelope so the easing shape is drawn by hand. Offsets, scale and spin lerp **unclamped** so Back/Elastic/a hand-drawn curve actually overshoot instead of being silently flattened; only alpha is clamped. (3) **`alpha`, `size` and `borderWidth` are `SplashScalar`s** — a ZUI `ZUIValue` "MultCont" (Static / Min-Max / Curve + the ⋯ multiplier menu) plus a **per-letter scope toggle** that chooses whether a curve reads the WHOLE line's life or each letter's own, so a staggered line can ripple. Curves are authored on a normalized 0..1 life (`OnValidate` pins their period to 1s), so a shape survives a change of hold. A per-letter `size` would re-flow the line, so it is applied as a per-quad scale about each glyph's centre instead of a point-size change. (4) **The border is a real filled outline.** `borderColor` becomes `borderFill`, a full `ZuiFill` (solid or gradient, same system as the face), and `borderWidth` is now a fraction of the font size with no 1.0 ceiling. This required abandoning TMP's built-in outline, which **cannot** do either: `TMP_Text.SetOutlineThickness` runs `Mathf.Clamp01(thickness)` (`TextMeshProUGUI.cs:1624`, `TextMeshPro.cs:1317`), so a width past 1 is impossible, and its colour is a single material uniform, so it could never carry a gradient. The border is instead a **duplicate TMP twin** drawn behind the face (a nested `Canvas` at `sortingOrder − 1` for UGUI — uGUI draws children AFTER their parent, so sorting is the only way to put a child behind it), whose FACE carries `borderFill`, and whose glyph quads are dilated. The dilation factor is normalised by `lineHeight ÷ quadHeight` so every glyph grows by the same absolute amount — a bare scale factor would give a short 'o' a visibly thinner border than a tall 'H'. It is still a uniform scale, not a true equidistant offset (a uniform scale cannot be one on a non-square shape); the exact version wants the render-texture path below. (5) **`SplashSchedule`**, a pure struct, now owns ALL timing (per-letter windows, `OutBase`, `Total`, `LetterLife`, `InProgress`/`OutProgress`), so the runtime player and the editor preview cannot drift. (6) **Pixelation that actually pixelates** — `SplashPixelRig` routes the splash Canvas through a dedicated orthographic camera into a `RenderTexture` of `ceil(screen ÷ pixelSize)` at `FilterMode.Point`, presented by an overlay `RawImage` sized to an exact integer multiple. Rasterization genuinely happens at that resolution (1080p at `pixelSize 4` is a real 480×270 image), rather than a shader faking chunky pixels — which is precisely what lets a CPU stack consume it. Plus `colorSteps` (channel quantization via a cached LUT) and `snapMotion` (motion rounded onto the pixel grid). An overlay Canvas composites outside the SRP, so the presenter works on Built-in, URP and HDRP alike — deliberately not `OnRenderImage` (never called under an SRP) nor a URP Renderer Feature (URP-only, needs renderer-asset config a package can't ship). (7) **SpriteFx runs on the pixelated buffer**, with no refactor needed: the Sprite coupling in SpriteFx lives entirely in the component, never in the stack API, so `SpriteFxStack.Resolve`/`Schedule` run straight over `Texture2D.GetRawTextureData<Color32>()` in place, zero copies. The rig forces the **Burst** path rather than honouring `SpriteFxSettings.UseBurstJobs`, whose inline default is justified for a 64×64 sprite frame (4,096 px) and is the wrong call for a buffer 8–30× larger. The window gains **Entrance** / **Exit** boxes built by one shared helper (so they cannot drift), scalar rows pairing a `Z.Value` with its scope toggle, a `Z.Vector2Field` 2D pad for the rest anchor (it was two sliders — a spatial pair, against the layout rules), and a **Pixelation** box. **Verified in-editor:** compiles clean; the window's schedule/pose math reproduces exact expected values at seven sample times (whole-line `OutBase` 1.85 / `Total` 2.2; per-letter with 8 letters and 0.05 stagger → `OutBase` 2.2 / `Total` 2.9, letter 0 window [0, 2.55] and letter 7 [0.35, 2.9], staggered in-progress 0.57 / 0.14 / 0 at t=0.20); `Back(0.5)` returns 1.088, confirming the overshoot survives; the border twin is created as the face's own concrete TMP type parented to the face, dilates the first glyph quad from 6.87 to 12.43 units at width 0.25, and correctly returns null at width 0; `ZuiAudit` reports **0 findings** with all 8 boxes open (a probe confirmed the 38 skipped subtrees are ZUI control internals — a `ZuiValueControl`'s Curve panel while it sits in Static, colour-field HDR chrome — not unaudited content). **NOT yet verified:** no Play-mode run, so `SplashPixelRig` is unexercised at runtime, and the window has not been eyeballed visually. **Known limitation:** TMP's SDF shader writes premultiplied alpha while the presenter blends `SrcAlpha OneMinusSrcAlpha`, so the pixelated splash's antialiased rim composites as `C·a²` — a faint one-pixel darkening at the edges, invisible on interior pixels and largely hidden by a dark border; the fix is a small premultiplied (`Blend One OneMinusSrcAlpha`) UI shader for the RawImage.
- **TextSplash — per-letter stagger + 3D spin, and fill colour-cycling (Slice 3).** Three new capabilities, all driven by the SAME runtime statics the preview uses. (1) **`perLetter`** animates each character independently: a `letterStagger` cascade (each letter starts its in/out that many seconds after the previous) plus a `spinDegrees` 3D spin about a chosen `spinAxis` (X/Y read as a foreshortened flip under the flat overlay camera, Z is in-plane), settling face-on at rest — the sequence length grows to cover the last letter's exit (`SequenceDuration(n)`). (2) **`cycleFill`** scrolls the FILL colour over time at `cycleSpeed` cycles/s — the **face only, the border is untouched** — and in per-letter mode `cyclePerLetter` offsets the phase from one letter to the next for a **travelling rainbow**. Implemented by manipulating the TMP mesh directly (`SplashPlayer.ApplyPerLetter` rotates each glyph quad about its own centre, offsets it, and recolours its vertices; `ApplyCycle` scrolls the whole-line vertex gradient), so it needs no extra GameObjects and runs identically in the runtime overlay and the editor's 3D-TMP preview. The window gains **Per-letter** (enable / stagger / spin° / axis) and **Colour cycle** (enable / speed / per-letter) sections. Verified in-editor: a "RAINBOW" splash cascades in with a letter caught mid-spin (an edge-on glyph foreshortens to a sliver) and rests as a travelling rainbow, over the BackSplash backdrop.
- **TextSplash — the authoring window with a live BackSplash preview (Slice 2).** `TextSplashWindow` (a `ZuiAssetWindow<TextSplash>`, opened from `Laubrary/Text Splash` or a LauAsset ✎ pen via `TextSplashEditorLink`) edits the whole splash on the left — text / font / size, the `ZuiFill` face, alpha, border colour + width, the three phase durations, and the transition (in-from / out-to edge as `Z.MiniRadio`, easing, rest anchor, slide distance) — and plays the full **in → hold → out** live on the right over a **BackSplash backdrop** (the same recallable colour/image stage Pyre and Mirage use, so a splash can be auditioned against a real background). ▶ Play loops it; the `t` scrubber steps through. The preview renders the splash through the SAME `SplashPlayer.ApplyLook` + `Evaluate` the runtime uses — via a **3D `TextMeshPro` in a `LiveScenePreview` isolated scene** (a UI-canvas TMP won't render under `PreviewRenderUtility`), with the mesh force-updated each repaint since that isolated scene never ticks `Update()`. `ApplyLook` was widened to take `TMP_Text` so the UGUI runtime text and the 3D preview text share one look-application path. Verified in-editor: "VICTORY!" renders with its fire-gradient face centred at hold and lower/rising from the bottom edge mid-slide, over the backdrop. (Slice 3: per-letter stagger + 3D spin + fill colour-cycling.)
- **TextSplash — a new "splash text on screen" LauAsset (Slice 1: runtime core).** `TextSplash` (a ScriptableObject LauAsset) carries everything to fly a line of text in, hold it, and fly it out: default text, per-transition durations, slide/fade direction + easing, a `ZuiFill` face (solid or gradient — reuses the ZUI fill system), a border (TMP outline), alpha, size and font. **One call plays it:** `splash.Show(overrideText = null, overrideHold = null)` — nulls use the asset's own defaults, either overrides just that play — which spawns a self-cleaning screen-overlay TMP, runs slide/fade **in → hold → out**, then despawns, returning a `SplashHandle` (await completion / cancel early). The look-application and pose are public statics (`SplashPlayer.ApplyLook` / `Evaluate`) so the editor preview drives the same animation. New `Laubrary.TextSplash` runtime module (asmdef → ZuiRuntime + PreviewKit + `Unity.TextMeshPro`). Verified in-editor: the pose evaluates correctly (off-screen bottom → rest → off-screen top → done) and `Show()` builds the overlay + TMP with the fill / border applied. (Slice 2: the authoring window with a BackSplash live preview; later — per-letter FX + fill colour-cycling.)
- **LauAsset adoption — real Pyre thumbnails + the picker across more tools (the "bigger task", #3 follow-through).** `Pyre` and `ChunkSpec` now implement `IVisualPreview`, so a picked Pyre shows a REAL blast thumbnail (via the same `BlastRenderer` its own tool uses — verified 64×64) and a ChunkSpec delegates to its animation source's preview (or a representative debris sprite), everywhere either is picked — not a generic asset icon. `Pyre` is registered with `LauAssetEditors` (Open → `PyreWindow`, Create) via a new `PyreEditorLink`, and `ChunkSpec` gains Create, so the ✎ Edit / New ▾ buttons light up on those fields. The obsolete `PyreChunksFxDrawer` is **retired**: an existing `PyreChunksFx`'s `blast` / `chunks` fields now flow through the LauAsset picker exactly like the new Spawn-Pyre / Spawn-Chunk palette effects, and its bespoke "Preview in Pyre" button is redundant with the ✎ pen. And the plain `ObjectField`s in the **Cartographer** (Clump / Biome / Tag / Recipe / room-biome / solid-tag — Clump & Biome get real inline thumbnails), **PyrePlus** import source, and **Pyre** preview-subject pickers are migrated to `LauAssetElement` (swatch + Recall… + New ▾ + ✎). Verified: Pyre renders a 64×64 thumbnail; ClumpWindow shows the LauAsset picker rows. Left as noted follow-ups: the dead IMGUI `LauAssetField.cs` (only referenced by throwaway test mockups, so deleting it needs those cleaned too); the IMGUI `ZoePreviewWindow` (LauAssetElement is UITK); registering the Cartographer types' Open/Create; and the Pyre preview-subject picker's `New ▾` breadth (constraint is `UnityEngine.Object`).
- **Zoetrope Target Practice — a clip-less death now shows NOTHING, not the frozen idle frame (combat batch #6).** When a Zoe's Death reaction has no clip, `TargetPracticeController` hides the body's SpriteRenderers for the dead interval instead of leaving it sitting on its last idle frame until respawn; `PlayIdle` re-shows them on revive. A Death that DOES play a clip keeps its final frame, as before.
- **Zoetrope Zoe-events — Hit Position can now Follow (sticks to the Zoe), and a Centre-Angle direction option (combat batch #4, #5).** (#4) The **Follow** toggle is no longer disabled for **Hit Position** — turning it on STICKS the effect to the Zoe: the hit point is captured in the Zoe's OWN space at spawn and re-projected every frame, so the effect rides along (translating + rotating with the Zoe) from exactly where the hit landed. (#5) `DirectionParam` gains **Centre Angle** (so the picker is None / Hit Direction / Centre Angle) — the effect is angled from its spawn point toward the Zoe's visual centre, so a side hit's fx points inward for a glancing look (`EventContext.ResolveDirectionDeg`). Appended last, so existing Hit-Direction / None values stay stable.
- **Zoetrope Zoe-events editor — mutable FX, a leaner effect palette + card, and the LauAsset picker for effect assets (combat batch).** (1) Each FX entry gains a **mute checkbox** in its card header (`FxEntry.enabled`, default on) — uncheck to keep the effect and its settings but stop it firing (the card dims); `ReactionFxPlayer.Fire` and `ReactionFx.Play` skip muted entries. (2) The combined **"Spawn Pyre Chunk"** (`PyreChunksFx`) is dropped from the Add-effect palette — Spawn Pyre + Spawn Chunk stack, so the bundle is redundant; the type stays so existing assets still deserialize. (3) The redundant foldable **"Effect"** sub-section inside each card is removed — the effect's fields draw inline (its kind is already named in the header), and because the palette kinds carry no custom `PropertyDrawer` their Pyre / Chunk asset fields now flow through `TryBuildAssetRefField` to the **LauAsset picker + preview** instead of a plain ObjectField. And `SpawnPyreFx` gains a **Direction** param (`Position | Direction | Scalar`) that ANGLES the spawned blast, so both Spawn-Pyre and Spawn-Chunk now expose Direction + Position. Verified in-editor on the palette demo: 4 LauAsset Recall-pickers, 4 mute toggles, and the only remaining managed-ref folds are the Zoe's own View / Brain.
- **PyrePlus — the shape-type picker moved into a header context menu.** The three in-body rows of shape-form radio buttons (3D / 2D / Special) are replaced by one context menu on the "Shape" section header, opened by a caret icon button next to the title OR by right-clicking the title — it lays the three groups out as **side-by-side columns** (3D / 2D / Special), each of the 16 forms an **icon + label** row (circle / diamond / moon / cube / sphere / flame / star / …) with the current one checked. Reclaims the vertical space the radio grid took. New reusable `ZuiSection.SetHeaderMenu(icon, tooltip, open)` adds the header button + the right-click wiring (the section still folds on a normal left-click). Picking a form does exactly what the radios did (same Dirty + `SteadyDefaultFillForSolid` + rebuild). Verified: 1 header button, 16 items, 3 sections, exactly 1 checked.
- **PyrePlus Crescent — the mask disc is now positioned by a 2D pad, replacing Offset + Angle (#12, part 2).** `crescentCenterXAnim` / `crescentCenterYAnim` — an animatable (x,y) pair in radius units (−1..1) over the particle's own life — replaces the polar Offset slider + Angle dial with a single `Z.Value2D` "Mask" pad ((0,0) = a centred hole/ring, push out for a thinner sliver). **Byte-identical:** the pair is null on existing assets, so the renderer uses the UNCHANGED polar path; the pad seeds its DISPLAY from the polar values but only WRITES the pair on an explicit drag — a silent polar→cartesian seed is not guaranteed byte-identical (`(off·radius)·cos` vs `(off·cos)·radius` differ by float-multiply order, and an animated-angle crescent would diverge outright). Verified: 3 crescent specs × 44 frames render identically through the new branch. (Editing the pad on an animated-angle crescent converts that angle animation into a static — but per-axis-animatable — centre.)
- **PyrePlus — the "Edge" (rim softness) control is now animatable over life (MultiCont) (#12, part 1).** `edgeSoftness` gains a `[SerializeReference] ZUIValue edgeSoftnessAnim` companion (Static / Min-Max / Curve over the particle's own life), so the soft rim can pulse or sharpen over life on every shape that shows the Edge control (Disc / Crescent / Star / Polygon / Ring / Streak — `EdgeRow` is shared). **Byte-identical migration:** a null companion falls back to the legacy `edgeSoftness` float — the shipped state, so existing assets render through the UNCHANGED float path — and the editor seeds it `Static(edgeSoftness)` on first build (Static evaluates to the legacy value). **Verified bit-for-bit: 31 specs × 575 frames, 0 pixel mismatches between the float path and the Static-seeded Eval path**, so the `0x2D95…` gate holds. (Part 2 — replacing the Crescent's Offset + Angle with a 2D mask-position pad — is still open: a naive polar→cartesian seed is NOT byte-identical because `(offset·radius)·cos` and `(offset·cos)·radius` differ by float-multiply ordering, so it needs a lazy convert-on-edit path rather than a silent migration.)
- **ZUI menu — Copy / Paste are now compact icon buttons, side by side (#8 follow-up).** New `ZuiMenu.IconRow(...)` adds a row of label-less icon buttons (the icon says it all, so no text — they stack horizontally and keep the row narrow). The ⋯ menus of `ZuiValueControl` and `ZuiValue2DControl` use it for **Copy** (copy glyph) + **Paste** (clipboard glyph) instead of two full-width text rows; Paste greys out on an empty clipboard. Icons verified to resolve.
- **ZUI MicroSlider display menu is now the DEFAULT on every slider (#10 follow-up).** The right-click display-options menu no longer needs an explicit `prefsKey` — when none is given the caption becomes the persistence key, so EVERY ZUI MicroSlider (the Pyre preview panel's, and the many Pyre dials that lacked it) gains the menu and remembers its toggles by name. An explicit key still wins, to disambiguate two same-named sliders. (Answer to "are they different sliders?" — no: they were the same `ZuiMicroSlider`, just built without a key.)
- **ZUI MicroSlider — an opt-in right-click "display options" menu on static sliders (#10).** `Z.MicroSlider` gains an optional `prefsKey`; when set, right-clicking the slider opens a stay-open ZUI menu with two persisted toggles — **Value in slider** (the in-track numeric readout) and **Numeric input** (an editable field to type an exact value, which replaces the in-track readout). Persisted in `EditorPrefs` per key — a stateless slider can't otherwise remember a toggle across ZUI's element rebuilds — so an un-keyed slider shows no menu and behaves exactly as before. First user: the gradient's Quantise slider (`grad.quantise`). Verified headlessly (the numeric field shows / hides and carries the live value as the two prefs flip).
- **ZUI fill — the mockup fill layout (objective + source ramps in the header, Adjust then Placement folding beneath); gradient envelopes gain a colour Y-axis (#1–4, #9).** `ZuiGradientEditor` splits the gradient editor into three COMPOSABLE pieces — `Output` (the objective preview strip, `ToLut()` with every transform baked in, read-only), `Source` (the editable base `GradientField`), and `Adjust` (the collapsible transforms box) — so a host can arrange them freely. `ZuiFillControl` now builds every gradient fill as a titled, bordered, collapsible "Fill" card whose HEADER carries [swatch square][Fill label][⋯ menu] plus the objective Output strip over the editable Source strip — all four stay visible when the fill folds (#3) — with the "Adjust" transforms and the spatial "Placement" box folding away in the body, Placement AFTER Adjust (#4). The two header ramps are the objective output on top (#2) and the ramp the user clicks to edit below it (#2) — verified rendering at 384×22 + 381×16. `ZuiGradientControl` (the standalone editor behind `Z.Gradient` / `ZuiReflect`) is now a thin composer stacking the same three pieces, so every call site gains the layout for free. **#9 — a colour Y-axis for envelopes:** `ZuiEnvelope` / `ZuiValueControl` gain `yColorFor` / `WithYColor` — a Curve envelope paints a vertical colour legend down its Y gutter and tints each control-point handle by the colour its value maps to; wired into the gradient's **Phase** transform (the Y-strip = the base ramp colour at each phase offset), verified end-to-end (the built 468×140 envelope receives the map, red→green across the range). The whole-ramp transforms (Hue / Saturation / Brightness / Contrast) deliberately get NO strip — they act on every stop at once, so a single "colour at value v" is undefined (pushback per the task's own invitation). UI-only; no serialized data changed; the `0x2D95…` render gate is untouched (all editor chrome). Still open on #1: the swatch square shows the fill/gradient but not the SHAPE silhouette — that needs the host to pass the shape into the fill control (it currently holds only the `ZuiFill`).
- **ZUI context menu — ZUI toggles instead of native checkboxes, wider rows, stay-open on click (#8).** `ZuiMenu`'s toggle rows now use `Z.ToggleButton` (the ZUI pill) instead of a native checkbox — a native checkbox in a ZUI popover was an unstated layout-guide violation, now CODIFIED as a rule (ZUI menus never use native checkbox chrome). Radio / toggle rows stop wrapping (`wrap:false`), so a row a hair too narrow widens the panel rather than breaking onto a second line. And the popover STAYS OPEN when a toggle inside it is clicked (a `PointerDownEvent` `StopPropagation` on the panel), so several options can be flipped in one visit instead of the menu closing on each click.
- **PyrePlus shape UI — a "Position" section on every shape, Solid / Light / Lines / Glow as siblings, the Advanced toggle gone (#11, #13).** The per-shape "Advanced" toggle is removed — sections collapse now, so a toggle gating extra controls was redundant clutter; the renderer already keyed travel off the path values being non-static-zero, never off the (now UI-less but still-serialized) `shapeAdvanced` flag, so **every render is byte-identical** (#11). The position-ish controls are grouped into one collapsible "Position" box present on EVERY shape (Turn / Tilt / Roll for the 3D solids, Spin for the flat forms, Offset for all) — a little sparse for the simple shapes but consistent — and "Travel" is renamed "Offset" since a static value causes no travel at all (#11). "Light", "Lines" and "Glow" are no longer nested inside "Solid": they are four sibling collapsible `Z.BoxKeyed` boxes (`pyreplus.solid` / `.light` / `.lines` / `.glow`), with Orb skipping the Solid box as before (#13). Editor-only; no asset or renderer change.
- **ZUI gradient — a "Phase" control to MOVE / animate the ramp with no shader (#5), + fixes (#6, #7).** `ZuiGradient` gains `phaseAnim` — a 0..1 scroll of the ramp, MultiCont (Static / Min-Max / Curve over life): set it to move the gradient, or animate it (a rising Curve) to scroll it over the particle's life without any shader; the shader/cycle driver's own phase adds on top. A non-zero scroll MIRRORS the ramp (0→1→0, via `PingPong`) so it loops SEAMLESSLY — no jump from the ramp's end back to its start. Byte-identical default (0 → no offset; re-verified 5904-sample differential, 0 mismatches). **#7:** the gradient's Adjust MultiCont transforms (Hue/Sat/Brightness/Contrast/Phase) now hide the wall-clock Duration/Warmup/Loop timing row and the Value-range row when set to Curve — they're sampled over the 0..1 life, not seconds, and their range is fixed, so those envelope extras were noise (they'd regressed into showing since the transforms became MultiCont; now they match Pyre's own `Val()`). **#6:** the fill's spatial-centre control renamed "Ctr" → "Centre". Cycle + Reverse packed onto one row (per the target mockup).
- **ZUI fill — the whole fill/gradient is now ONE titled, bordered, collapsible "Fill" section.** `ZuiFillControl` wraps every multi-control fill (the gradient modes and every texture face) in a titled `Z.Box` named by the fill's label, carrying the live swatch + the ⋯ mode menu in its header, with the gradient's "Adjust" transforms and the spatial "Placement" as clearly-nested subsections inside it. So it is unmistakable where the fill section starts (its title) and ends (its bottom border) — a host's own dials stacked after it (Alpha / Size / Edge / …) read as separate, not as more of the fill — and "Placement" reads correctly as *the fill's* placement now that it sits under "Fill". A Solid fill is one colour field and stays inline (the guide: never title a box holding one field).
- **ZUI gradient — animatable (MultiCont) colour transforms + a collapsible "Adjust" sub-section; fill placement bounded.** `ZuiGradient`'s four colour transforms (hue / saturation / brightness / contrast) are now **ZUIValue (MultiCont)** — Static / Min-Max / Curve over the particle's 0..1 life, so brightness can pulse or hue can drift over life just like a fill's Alpha/Size/Spin already do. **Byte-identical migration** (the legacy floats are the frozen serialized source; `[SerializeReference]` ZUIValue companions seed `Static(legacy)` on load ⇒ `Evaluate` reads them unchanged; a null companion falls back to the legacy float — the exact pattern ZuiFill uses for zoom/centre). `Evaluate`/`ToLut` gain a `life` param, and `ZuiFill.EvalGrad` threads the shape's life through so the transforms animate. **Verified bit-for-bit identical to the old float math across 5904 samples (0 mismatches), so the `0x2D95…` render gate holds.** The live GPU recolour shader bakes its LUT from a life-0 snapshot (animated transforms are a CPU/baked-path feature). Editor: `ZuiGradientControl` now shows an always-visible preview + base ramp, with the transforms in a collapsible **"Adjust"** box (Hue/Sat/Brightness/Contrast as `Z.Value` MultiCont, each with the ⋯ Static/Min-Max/Curve menu; Quantise stays an int MicroSlider — an animated band count reads as flicker). `ZuiFillControl` puts the spatial placement (Anchor / Zoom / Centre) in its OWN collapsible **"Placement"** box instead of a bleeding `Z.Divider`, so a fill is a bounded unit and a host's own dials (Alpha / Size / Edge / …) sit clearly outside it — closing the "gradient controls mixed with other things under Placement" confusion.
- **ZUI gradient editor — envelope-style collapse-with-preview, consolidated onto ONE shared control; Pyre fill sectioned.** `ZuiGradientControl` now collapses like a ZUI envelope: the live `ToLut()` preview strip is the always-visible fold HEADER, and the base gradient + transform knobs (reverse / hue / sat / brightness / contrast / quantise / cycle) fold away beneath it — so a stack of gradient groups no longer reads as a wall of controls, yet the ramp itself stays visible when collapsed. The fold is delegated to the shared `ZuiFoldCard` (caret + `Clickable`, per-instance persisted state) so it survives window rebuilds. A new optional `collapsible = true` constructor param (default on; pass `false` for the old flat layout) means every existing call site — `Z.Gradient`, `ZuiReflect`'s `ZuiGradient` branch, and the ColorRemap region editors — gains collapse-with-preview for free, with no call-site changes. `ZuiFillControl` now routes every gradient mode (OverLife / Linear / Radial / Noise-texture) through that ONE shared control instead of a raw `GradientField`, which also closes a real gap — a fill's `ZuiGradient` transform knobs were previously unauthorable even though the runtime samples them (`ZuiFill.EvalGrad`) — and delimits the gradient from the spatial placement controls with a labelled `Z.Divider` ("Placement" / "Pattern"), the explicit "gradient ends here" boundary. UI-only; no serialized data model changed. Verified: compiles clean; the shared control builds both collapsible and flat; the recolour region editors still build unaffected (2 swatch + 2 gradient controls). (`ZuiFill.EvalGrad` samples at phase 0, so a fill's `cycle` toggle is authored-but-inert until a phase driver exists — consistent with reusing the one control.)
- **ZUI colour (#77 live GPU path) — `SpriteColorRemap` shader + `ColorRemapGpu` driver (spike-verified).** `Zui/Shaders/SpriteColorRemap.shader` is a URP sprite shader that runs the ColorRemap recolour on the GPU: up to 8 regions pushed as uniforms, each matching a hue cluster (by HUE window) or a grey range (by VALUE window) and mapping to a flat swatch OR a luma-indexed gradient sampled from a per-region LUT ATLAS (one row per region); cycling regions scroll their row by `_Phase`; unmatched colours pass through (or drop). `Runtime/SpriteFx/ColorRemapGpu.cs` (`[ExecuteAlways]` driver) bakes a `ColorRemapModifier`'s regions into those uniforms + the atlas, assigns the material, and advances the phase over time — free colour CYCLING with no per-frame CPU pixel work; re-bakes lazily on an edit or a watched `SwatchPalette` version bump. `ColorRemapGpuEditor` renders the same region table as the CPU applier (via `ZuiReflect`), hiding the two flags the GPU path ignores (`enabled`, `applyTargetAlpha`). **Spike-verified against the design's gated phase 4: a real URP render of the shader diffs to mean 0.0003 per channel vs the managed `SpriteFxRecolor` output, and the driver end-to-end matches to the same 0.0003.** URP-only; the match math mirrors `ColorRemapRegion.TryScore`.
- **ZUI colour (#77 editor + #73 swatch picker) — ColorRemap is now authorable, and `Z.Swatch` lands.** `SpriteFxRecolor` gets a ZUI inspector (`Editor/SpriteFx/SpriteFxRecolorEditor.cs`) that reuses `SpriteFxStackView` with a new `includeColorRemap` opt-in — a managed host runs every `PixelModifier` via `ApplyPixel`, so it may offer the non-shaped Colour-remap effect the triggered op-path filter skips — and the region table renders through `ZuiReflect`. New `ZuiSwatchControl` + `Z.Swatch(ZuiSwatchRef,…)` is **#73's content-swatch picker**: palette ObjectField + swatch-name dropdown + inline colour + a live RESOLVED preview. `ZuiReflect` now also renders `ZuiGradient` (the transform-knobs class, via `ZuiGradientControl`) and `ZuiSwatchRef` (via `Z.Swatch`), so every reflected tool gains both at once — closing the last gaps for a clean recolour editor. (Noted while here: `ZuiReflect` already rendered enum / Color / string / Gradient / Object / `List<T>` — the CLAUDE.md "color-field / enum-popup / text-input gap" notes are STALE.) `SpriteFxRecolor`'s runtime work texture + sprite now carry `HideFlags.DontSave` (it is `[ExecuteAlways]`, so it must never serialise into a saved scene). Verified headlessly: the inspector builds for a 2-region remap with 2 swatch pickers + 2 gradient controls + 4 colour fields, no exceptions.
- **ZUI colour (#77) — recolour existing sprites/Reels: `ColorRemapModifier` + `SpriteFxRecolor` (static path).** A managed `PixelModifier` (`Runtime/SpriteFx/SpriteFxColorRemap.cs`) that maps a source-colour cluster — a hue with all its shades, or a grey range — to either a flat `ZuiSwatchRef` swatch or a **luma-indexed `ZuiGradient`** (the pixel's own brightness picks the ramp position, so a shaded region's shading is PRESERVED, never flattened to one tone). It keys on **colour, not pixel position**, so it follows an animated Reel automatically with no per-frame masks; colours outside every region pass through untouched (or drop, opt-in). `SpriteFxRecolor` is the always-on managed applier (`[ExecuteAlways]` component) that re-reads a `SpriteRenderer`'s live pixels each frame and swaps in the recoloured result — the design's "SpriteFx CPU filter" delivery, riding a live Reel/Animator the same way `SpriteFxFilter` does. Deliberately NOT in `SpriteFxStack.IsShaped`, so the triggered Burst/op filter skips it cleanly and only the managed `ApplyPixel` path runs it; the live GPU sprite-LUT shader + colour-cycling path (a LUT-baked shaped op) is the follow-up. Verified headlessly (hue/grey matching, flat-swatch, gradient-by-luma shade preservation, unmatched passthrough, deep-clone independence) and end-to-end through the component with a before/after render (shaded blue→orange gradient with shading intact, flat green→purple swatch, grey outline untouched). Region-table editor authoring lands next.
- **Cartographer — Phases 2–5 completed: procgen, camera modes, and a family-aware rule tile.** Closes out every buildable item on the tool's phase list. **Procgen (Phase 3):** **`LevelRecipe`** (asset) is seed-plus-dials describing a KIND of level — biome, width, ground clump and how much the surface wanders, weighted scatter entries with per-100-cell density and height bands, and the rooms to copy on — an asset so several Sites can share "ruined street" without duplicating dials. **`LevelGenerator.Generate(recipe, seed, level)`** is deterministic from `System.Random(seed)` and is a **composer, not a painter**: every clump goes through `PlaceClump`, so a generated level carries the same placement records, publishes the same named spots, and rebuilds the same way as a hand-built one — the single runtime representation the whole design was built around. The biome **gates** scatter (a clump it disallows is never placed, however the dials are set), a crowded scatter roll is **dropped rather than retried** (retrying skews density toward whatever fits), and the generator passes its own seeded picker into `PlaceClump` so a clump's prefab alternatives are chosen from the level seed — regenerating a seed picks the same variants. A Generate box (recipe / seed / Roll / Generate) drives it from the window and builds colliders afterwards. **Camera modes (Phase 5):** a per-Room `RoomCamera` — **Follow** (the director's view centre), **Rail** (an authored path sampled by `RoomProgress`, independent of the player, for a set-piece approach) and **Focus** (locked to a target until the room ends). Exposed as `RoomDirector.CameraPosition`, deliberately separate from `ViewCenter` so a Rail or Focus camera looks elsewhere WITHOUT disturbing the scroll or the catch-up rules; an un-authored rail falls back to the ordinary view rather than teleporting to the origin. The old "player lock / clock anchor" idea lands as a per-Room **`playerLead`** from −1 (trailing edge, so nearly the whole view is what lies ahead) through 0 to +1 — a clock face was considered and rejected, since along a single scroll axis it is one number, not two. It fixes a real flaw: player-pushed scroll previously hardcoded the player at the *leading* edge and therefore showed you everything behind you. **`CartographerRuleTile` (Phase 2):** tiles carry a `family` string and any two sharing one satisfy each other's neighbour rules, so a roof interior, a roof edge and three variants stop needing to list each other by hand; the ordinary This/Not-this buttons honour the family too. `RuleTile` ships in the **optional** `com.unity.2d.tilemap.extras` package, so it is guarded by a `TILEMAP_EXTRAS_INSTALLED` versionDefine — the same pattern Zounds uses for Addressables — and Cartographer never forces that dependency: the type compiles away where the package is absent (this repo) and is live where it is present. **Verified in edit mode throughout**, by driving the code directly rather than entering play: Rail sampling to (0,50) at progress 0.5, Focus resolving to its target, `playerLead −0.5` putting the view at `player + 0.5×extent`, the biome refusing a disallowed clump 0 times out of ~30 rolls, and determinism checked **by hashing the actual placement list** — 1234 == 1234, and 1234 ≠ 9999 ≠ 1. Worth recording that the first determinism check compared placement *counts* and wrongly suggested the seed did nothing: two different levels place the same number of clumps constantly, so counts are not evidence.
- **Cartographer — Phase 5: Rooms actually run. `RoomDirector` + `RoomCameraBinder` turn a built level into one you move through.** The Room schema (exit conditions, scroll behaviours, `ScrollCatchUp`, reserved areas) had been designed and authored into `CartographerLevel` but nothing consumed any of it — a level could be built and looked at, never traversed. **`RoomDirector`** advances through `level.rooms`, evaluating each one's exit — **Distance**, **Time**, or **ClearEnemies**, the last asking a `Func<bool> RoomCleared` the *game* supplies, since Cartographer has no idea what an enemy is (left null it ends immediately rather than hanging forever) — and raising `RoomStarted` / `LevelFinished`. **Scroll behaviours**: Auto advances the view at the room's speed; Locked holds it; PlayerPushed moves the view only forward and only as far as the player has actually pushed it. **The `ScrollCatchUp` rules run**: Lock and Push pin the player to the trailing edge (Push is where a game's crush check belongs — Cartographer will not guess at that); Wait hands the distance back so an advancing view never leaves anyone behind; Kill raises `PlayerCaught` **on the crossing**, latched — the first test run showed it firing once per tick while the player stayed behind the edge, which would tell a game it had died dozens of times. **`RoomCameraBinder`** points a Camera at `ViewCenter` with optional exponential smoothing, kept a separate component on purpose: the director decides where play is *looking*, which is equally useful to a minimap, a cutscene, or a test with no camera at all. **Rooms became authorable** — a Rooms box on the Level box adds sections and edits name / biome / exit condition (showing only the parameter that exit actually uses) / scroll / speed / catch-up; until now the Room schema was unreachable from any UI. **Built as a tickable on purpose:** the director is `Tick(dt, playerPos)` applied to its own state, with `Update` merely calling it, so every behaviour above is verified in EDIT mode by driving `Tick` directly — no play mode, no waiting on wall-clock time, no flaky timing. Verified that way: a two-room sequence advancing A(Distance 20 @ speed 4, ends at exactly 5s and view x=20) → B(Time 3s, Locked, view provably stationary) → `LevelFinished`; Lock pinning the player at exactly `view.x − 8` (the view extent); Wait holding the view at `player + 8`; PlayerPushed leaving the view still when the player idles and trailing to `player − 8` when they advance; ClearEnemies holding until the supplied predicate flips; and `PlayerCaught` raised once, not once per tick.
- **Cartographer — the Stamper, level scaffolding and collision: it builds real levels now. Developed inside OutBurner's embedded copy and promoted back.** Phases 2 and most of 4, built where the tool is actually used so the ergonomics surfaced immediately — and they did, which is most of what this entry is. **A Level box** in the Clump Editor scaffolds a level (a `Grid`, Terrain and Structures tilemaps, the component wired, one undo step) and manages its layers — name, `solid`, `oneWay`, collider shape — plus the collision mode and the solid tag. **No new menu commands**: creation is a button in the window, per the house rule that the asset browser owns creation. **The Stamper** ghosts the clump's transformed footprint at the hovered cell and stamps on click, with rotation (0/90/180/270), mirror-X, and Alt-click to remove the placement under the cursor; everything places through `CartographerLevel.PlaceClump`, the same call a generator will use, so hand-built and generated levels stay one code path. **Drag-to-repeat and line mode** answer the tool's worst friction — a flat ground run cost one click per clump width. Drag-to-repeat uses **overlap rejection** rather than one-stamp-per-cell (an 8-wide strip clump would otherwise pile eight overlapping copies where one belongs), so a drag tiles clumps edge-to-edge in any direction while a 1×1 clump still stamps every cell; line mode presses, drags out an axis-locked run and commits on release, with the ghost and the commit sharing one `LineOrigins` so the preview cannot drift from what lands. **Placements are recorded** — `(clump, origin, rotation, mirror)` — because a Tilemap only stores tiles, so once a clump is stamped the fact that those cells came from THAT clump is gone, and with it its tags, its prefab hook and any way to rebuild; `RebuildFromPlacements()` re-stamps the lot, and erase drops a placement and rebuilds rather than clearing a footprint an overlapping neighbour may share. **Collision** (`BuildColliders`, idempotent so it doubles as a rebuild button): solid layers get a `TilemapCollider2D` merged into a `CompositeCollider2D` over a static body; one-way layers add a `PlatformEffector2D`, but only in side-scroll mode, since in a top-down level "up" is not a direction you fall from; a per-layer collider shape gives **slopes** (sprite-outline collision means a triangular tile collides on its own shape — no custom geometry); and an optional **`solidTag`** narrows a solid layer to cells from clumps carrying it, so decorative clumps can share a solid layer without blocking. That last resolved an open design question in favour of tags **narrowing a solid layer and never widening a non-solid one**, so there is exactly one place to look when something unexpectedly blocks; it is applied per CELL, not per tile asset, because the same Ground tile can be structural in one clump and dressing in another. **An inline Biome editor** sits beside the palette it feeds (name, tint, terrain tiles, allowed clumps) rather than being a second window, which would have needed its own menu entry. **Two bugs building a real level caught, both invisible to the mechanical audit:** a clump cell targeting a layer the level lacks was skipped **silently** — layer names are free text on both sides, so a typo dropped tiles with no trace — and now warns naming the clump, the missing layer and the level's actual layers; and the brush's layer list was derived only from the clump's own cells, so a level with a `Platforms` layer did not offer it when authoring a new clump (it now unions the target level's layers, which matters precisely because painting onto a layer the level lacks drops those cells). **Verified in-editor throughout:** a real side-scrolling test level built with the tool (13 placements, 3 layers, colliders); a 48-cell drag yielding exactly 6 placements at origins 0/8/16/24/32/40; rotation provable (a 4×2 footprint becoming 2×4) and mirroring reaching spots (a mirrored stamp's spot resolving to 20 + (−1.5) + 0.5 = 19.0); `RebuildFromPlacements` reproducing tile counts with no duplication; tagged-vs-untagged clumps colliding 8 vs 8; and `ZuiAudit` 0 findings — its 4 `foldedSkipped` confirmed by walking the tree to be Unity's own hidden `ColorField` chrome from the new tint field, with `ExpandAll` opening 0, so nothing of ours went unaudited.
- **Cartographer — Phase 2 begins: the Clump Editor, a paint-on-a-grid authoring window, so a Clump stops being a hand-typed list of cell offsets.** `Editor/Cartographer/ClumpWindow.cs`, opened from `Laubrary/Cartographer/Clump Editor` (or `ClumpWindow.OpenFor(clump)`, the same entry-point shape `PyreWindow`/`MirageWindow` expose for a field's Edit button). Built on **`ZuiAssetWindow<Clump>`** — so the asset toolbar (assign/New/Duplicate/Rename/Delete/Browse) and the thumbnail browser come free, the browser grid animating the `IVisualPreview` thumbnails Phase 1 added — and follows `ChoreographerWindow`'s shape as the UI Toolkit reference: a fixed-width left column of dials in a `ScrollView`, a **`Painter2D` custom element** for the stage, and a single `Dial(label, apply)` helper every data edit routes through, so the whole window is undoable in one place. **Left column:** the display name; a **Palette** that seeds itself from a chosen Biome's terrain tiles *and* from whatever tiles the clump already uses (so an existing asset is paintable the moment it opens, rather than needing its own tiles re-added by hand), plus a direct tile field for one-offs, drawn as clickable sprite swatches with the active brush ringed; a **Brush** box choosing the target layer (`Z.MiniRadio` over Terrain/Structures plus any other layer the clump already writes to, and a text field to start a new one) and Paint-vs-Erase; a **Grid** box sizing the visible painting area; and **Tags** and **Spots** lists. **The stage** draws the clump's REAL tile sprites — Painter2D cannot draw a texture, so the tiles are a pool of `Image` children (the same trick Choreographer's stage uses for its sample sprites), ordered Terrain-then-Structures so they composite the way the level stacks its layers. Click or drag to paint; hold Alt or use the right button to erase; painting the same cell+layer replaces rather than stacking, and a drag that re-enters the cell it is already on is skipped so one gesture doesn't fill the undo stack. **Two bugs found by looking at it, not by the audit:** the palette never populated on build (`BuildControls` rebuilt layers/tags/spots but not the palette), and the origin marker + spot rings were buried under the tiles — Painter2D content draws BENEATH an element's children, so those markers moved onto a `pickingMode = Ignore` overlay child kept in front via `BringToFront()`, with a dark backing ring so a marker reads against any tile colour. **Verified in-editor at a realistic 1000×620:** compiles clean; `ZuiAudit` returns **0 findings with `foldedSkipped == 0`** (authoritative); and a window capture confirms by eye that the Hut loads with both its tiles in the palette, its two wall rows and roof course in the right cells, the origin cell outlined, and the `hut-door` spot ring landing exactly on the 1.5-cell boundary its offset specifies. **Still Phase 2:** the Clump Stamper (place a clump onto a scene Tilemap) and the Biome CRUD window; the Scavenger sprite-sourcing question stays deliberately open, since painting from tiles already in the project needs none of it.
- **ZUI (#72) — the UITK control context menus converted from raw `GenericMenu` to the `Z.Menu` popover framework, so every ⋯ / add / type-picker menu in a UI-Toolkit window is now an in-panel, ZUI-styled flyout instead of an OS-drawn menu.** The follow-on rollout of the `Z.Menu`/`ZuiPopover` framework (task #68) across the UITK surface — a pure UI-shell swap, behaviour-preserving (same choices, same actions, same Undo), touching no renderer or data. Each `MenuButton` that used to call `menu.ShowAsContext()` now self-anchors (captures its own `Button` and passes it to `ShowMenu(anchor)`) so the popover opens attached to the ⋯ / add button it belongs to. **The four `Zui/Toolkit/` controls:** **`ZuiValueControl`** (the animatable-value "MultCont") — mode (Static / Min-Max / Curve) → a `.Radio` group with `closeOnSelect` (a "Mode" section heading appears only when a multiplier exists, matching the old "Mode/" prefix); the Curve-only display options (Show point values / Show numeric inputs / Inputs for selected points only) → `.Toggle` rows that STAY OPEN so several can be flipped in one visit (they used to be close-on-click checkmark items); the multiplier choice ("(none)" + each id) → its own `.Section` + a stay-open `.Radio`; Copy/Paste → `.Item`s after a `.Separator` (Paste disabled when the clipboard doesn't parse). **`ZuiValue2DControl`** — Static/Animate mode → a `closeOnSelect` `.Radio` (only when the source animates), the two display toggles → stay-open `.Toggle` rows (static view only), Reset + Copy/Paste → `.Item`s. **`ZuiFillControl`** — the two-section fill/texture menu → a `.Section("Fill")` (Solid / Over life / Linear / Radial, the active one `@checked`) + a `.Separator()` + a `.Section("Texture")` (Sprite / Noise / Grid / Dots), the "Texture · " label prefix dropped now that the heading names the group. **`ZuiManagedRef`** (the `[SerializeReference]` type picker used by every reflected effect/modifier stack incl. the Zoe-events editor) — its `TypeCache`-derived concrete-type list → flat `.Item`s (None + each type, current `@checked`), assignment + Undo (`ApplyModifiedProperties`) unchanged. **Sweep — the other UITK windows' add/picker menus:** `ChunkWindow` (+ Add modifier), `PyreWindow` (+ Add modifier — its `Geometry/Colour/Alpha/Post` slash-submenus become flat `.Section` headings; and the preview Attach-id picker), `PyrePlusWindow` (+ Add simulation; the + Add modifier menu was already `Z.Menu` since #68), `SpriteFxStackView` (+ Add SpriteFx), `LauAssetElement` (New ▾), and `MirageWindow` (New… asset-create + Add-clip-step). **Deliberately LEFT as `GenericMenu` (IMGUI contexts a UITK popover cannot render into):** all of legacy `Zui/Scripts/Editor/` IMGUI ZUI (`ZUIValueControl`, `ZUIValue2DControl`, `ZUIFoldControls`, `ZUIContextMenu`, `ZUIPolymorphicFoldout`, `ZUIStyleEditorWindow`, `ZUIAutoColorEditor`, `ZUITextureEditor`, `ZUIDebug`); every `Zounds/*` window (`GenericMenuPopup`/`GenericMenuEditorWindow`, `BrowserTab`, `RoutingTab`, `ZoundInspector`, `ZequenceEditorWindow`, `RenderZequenceToKlipPopup`, `CompositeZoundEditorWindow`); `Launimator`'s `AnimationBuilderWindow`(+`.AutoSlice`); `LazorWindow.Canvas` (`LaubraryAssetWindow`, IMGUI); the IMGUI `LauAssetField` (twin of the converted `LauAssetElement`); `LaunimatorZounds.ZoundPickerPopup` (wraps Zounds' IMGUI `GenericMenuPopup`); and the runtime `MirageAssetPicker.BuildMenu` (consumed by `MirageHud`'s in-game `OnGUI`). **Verified in-editor:** compiles clean (every converted call site); driven live in PyrePlus at 820px — a `ZuiValueControl` ⋯ opens the anchored ZUI menu (mode radio + Copy/Paste items, no curve toggles in Static), picking "Curve over time" flips `ZUIValue.mode` Static→Curve AND closes the menu, and the "Show point values" toggle flips its flag and the menu STAYS open; `ZuiFillControl` shows the Fill+Texture sections with the active fill checked; `ZuiValue2DControl` shows mode-radio + 2 display toggles + Reset + Copy/Paste; in the Zoe editor a `ZuiManagedRef` type button opens the menu listing None + all four view types (current checked) and the item's `Assign` reassigns the `[SerializeReference]` (ZonedReelView→CompositeReelView); `ZuiAudit` on PyrePlus returns **0 findings** on the visible chrome. No committed `.asset` changed.
- **Story — ships a working `IRuleBridge` at last: `RulesHostRuleBridge` + `InjectRulePage`, so a Screenplay can actually read and mutate live game rules.** Story has declared `IRuleBridge` (`StoryTypes.cs`) and consumed it (`Page.Rules`, `StoryRunner.RuleBridge`) since it was written, but shipped **zero implementations** — every consumer had to supply its own, and `Pages.cs`'s own comment ("Games extend this with bespoke pages, e.g. TrueEye's InjectRulePage") records that expectation. **`RulesHostRuleBridge`** implements the interface over Rulesets' own `RulesHost`: rules addressed by type NAME, parameters by public field name via reflection, so it needs no game-specific knowledge whatsoever — `IsActive`/`SetActive`/`GetParam`/`SetParam` plus an `Inject` that adds a live `GameRule` through `RulesHost.AddLive` and returns an `IDisposable` which removes it again and, if it displaced a single-instance rule of the same type, restores that rule's previous active state. **`InjectRulePage`** is the matching Page: it drops a fully-authored `GameRule` into the live host when a bookmark reaches it, **cloning through `JsonUtility` first** so a running Screenplay never mutates its own authored asset, and registering the returned disposable with the runner so the injection unwinds on teardown (`GraphRunner.RegisterDisposable`, disposed in reverse order — the mechanism was already there and unused). **No new dependency:** `Story`'s asmdef already referenced `Rulesets`, which is why `IRuleBridge` lives in Story to begin with — so this needed no bridge module and adds no coupling that wasn't already declared. **Rescued from the retired RulesAgentLab scratch project**, where it had been written as a deliberately de-game-ified rewrite of TrueEye's game-specific pair (its own header: *"kept dependency-free so it's copy-paste portable into Runtime/Story once proven here"*) and then never promoted. **Verified in-editor:** compiles clean, and a reflection probe confirms `RulesHostRuleBridge` lands in `com.Lautaro-Arino.Laubrary.Story` implementing `IRuleBridge`, and `InjectRulePage` extends `Page` as an `IGraphNode`/`INode`. **Not yet exercised at runtime** — no Screenplay in this repo injects a rule, so the inject/dispose round trip has compiled but never run; the RulesDemo scene that drove it was deliberately not rescued.
